# ATD Helpdesk System Overview

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source branch:** `feature/launch-communications-queue-compatibility`
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Status:** Foundational system reference
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-1-SCRIPT-586`

## 1. Purpose

ATD Helpdesk is an internal service-management portal that brings employee support, ticket operations, access governance, communications, administration, reporting, and selected enterprise integrations into one controlled application.

The system is designed for several audiences:

- Employees who submit and follow requests.
- Helpdesk agents who triage, assign, investigate, communicate, and resolve work.
- Managers who monitor queues, team performance, approvals, and operational risk.
- Administrators who manage users, roles, permissions, configuration, communications, and governance.
- Superadministrators who perform high-impact governed actions.
- Developers and operational support staff who maintain, validate, deploy, and troubleshoot the platform.

## 2. Core product areas

### 2.1 Authentication and account lifecycle

The application contains sign-in, registration, approval, terms acceptance, onboarding, profile, avatar, role, and permission foundations. Access to protected functions must be enforced by the backend and reflected by the frontend.

### 2.2 Ticket management

The ticketing area supports the operational lifecycle from request creation through triage, assignment, work, communication, resolution, closure, reopening, archive, restore, and governed deletion. Supporting functions include attachments, activity history, comments, participants, bulk operations, SLA information, and queue views.

### 2.3 Employee experience

Employees use the portal to create and review requests, follow updates, respond to support, view announcements, access knowledge and catalogue functions, manage profile settings, and use authorised dashboard experiences.

### 2.4 Agent and manager operations

Operational users work with queues, ticket details, assignment acceptance, support groups, priorities, statuses, notifications, SLA information, activity history, dashboards, and reporting foundations.

### 2.5 Administration and governance

Administrative functions include user administration, access requests, roles and permissions, temporary entitlements, support groups, email governance, communications, directory integration, archive controls, health evidence, and audit information.

### 2.6 Company Communications

Company Communications provides campaign authoring, audience definition, approval, scheduling, attachments, announcements, conversations, delivery history, and email-governance integration. The asynchronous delivery-reliability batch remains in progress and must not be documented as completed until Script 582 or its guarded replacement is validated.

### 2.7 Dashboards, themes, and layouts

The portal contains dashboard templates, widgets, pinned actions, employee and operational dashboard experiences, appearance preferences, and layout foundations.

Glassmorphism is a **portal theme and layout styling option only**. It must be managed with themes, Light/Dark mode, accessibility safeguards, and named custom layouts. Glassmorphism does not apply to email templates.

### 2.8 Knowledge, catalogue, assets, and workspaces

The repository contains foundations for knowledge content, request catalogue and guided routing, asset or configuration records, department workspaces, and related administrative experiences. Documentation must use the current product name for asset/configuration functionality once the approved replacement for “CMDB” is supplied. Functionality and data must not be removed during rebranding.

### 2.9 Notifications, activity, and audit

The platform records operational events and exposes notification and activity experiences. Action-required items must remain actionable after being marked read. Ticket activity should identify participant, activity, and timestamp.

### 2.10 Integrations

The system contains integration foundations for Microsoft Graph, SMTP email, Microsoft directory synchronisation, inbound email-to-ticket, Freshservice historical data, and production reporting or synchronisation services.

## 3. Capability status language

All documentation in this repository must use one of these states:

- **Implemented and deployed**
- **Implemented and validated locally**
- **Implemented locally, validation pending**
- **Partially implemented**
- **Foundation present**
- **Planned**
- **Deferred**
- **Legacy or retained for compatibility**
- **Unable to verify**

Source presence alone does not prove production availability. Deployment evidence and production validation are required before a capability is marked implemented and deployed.

## 4. Current evidence notes

The documentation scan was collected from a working tree that contained existing local modifications. These local files must not automatically be described as deployed. Historic release packages, reports, backups, and archived source are secondary evidence and do not override current `frontend/src`, `backend/src`, current migrations, or verified production evidence.

## 5. Product principles

- Preserve working functionality and data.
- Apply backend authorisation to every governed action.
- Use minimal, reversible changes with backups and rollback.
- Record significant activity with actor and timestamps.
- Keep test email recipients isolated from live audiences.
- Make high-impact actions explicit and governed.
- Provide accessible, responsive interfaces.
- Keep roadmap items separate from completed functionality.
- Validate a completed milestone comprehensively before deployment.

## 6. Related documents

- [Functional Capability Guide](02_FUNCTIONAL_CAPABILITY_GUIDE.md)
- [Technical Architecture](03_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md)
- [Roles, Access and Governance](04_ROLES_ACCESS_AND_GOVERNANCE.md)


END OF FILE: 01_SYSTEM_OVERVIEW.md

# ATD Helpdesk Functional Capability Guide

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-1-SCRIPT-586`

## 1. Reading this guide

This guide describes functional areas confirmed by current repository evidence. It intentionally avoids treating every source file as a deployed feature. Detailed validation and deployment status will be reconciled in later documentation groups and the capability register.

## 2. Authentication and profile

### Available foundations

- User sign-in and account lifecycle handling.
- Registration and approval foundations.
- Terms or policy acceptance.
- First-login onboarding.
- Current-user profile retrieval and update.
- Secure profile media and avatar handling.
- Role and permission-aware navigation and access.

### Governance expectations

- The backend remains authoritative for access.
- Avatar changes must refresh consistently and provide a fallback.
- Existing accounts with no directory changes must not be written unnecessarily.

## 3. Ticket lifecycle

### Core functions

- Create and view tickets.
- Track requestor, department, priority, type, support group, assignee, status, dates, and SLA information.
- Assign and accept assignments.
- Add requester-visible replies and internal notes.
- Record ticket activity and participant timestamps.
- Resolve and close tickets.
- Reopen resolved tickets when an issue reappears.
- Attach and preview supported files.
- Perform governed bulk operations.
- Archive, restore, and permanently delete through controlled lifecycle rules.

### Required reporting behaviour

Reopened incidents must be separately counted and must support repeat-issue indicators in management reporting.

## 4. Ticket workspaces and queues

The source includes ticket list and detail experiences, filtering, dashboard queues, priority views, archive views, and assignment panels. Broad filtering is expected by department, requestor, priority, incident type, and service-request type. Bulk actions must also operate in the Dashboard Ticket Priority queue.

List/Kanban workspace, adaptive insight panels, saved views, and live refresh must be classified against actual implementation evidence before being presented as complete.

## 5. SLA capability

SLA information is associated with ticket priority and operational handling. Syspro/ERP initial-response targets are:

- Critical: 30 minutes
- High: 3 hours
- Medium: 24 hours
- Low: 48 hours

These are response targets, not resolution guarantees. Infrastructure retains its separate SLA matrix.

## 6. Notifications and activity

### Confirmed foundations

- Persistent notification records.
- Read and unread management.
- Action-required notifications.
- Ticket activity and assignment actions.
- Access-request actions.
- Filtering and clear-completed safeguards.

### Product requirement

Marking a notification as read must not remove or disable a pending governed action. Compact floating change notifications with an Undo option are a priority interaction pattern. Expanded notification history and a dedicated Activity view may be delivered separately.

## 7. Company Communications

### Functional scope

- Campaign authoring and editing.
- Audience selection and exclusions.
- Whole-company, department, management-level, combined position-level, and external-recipient targeting.
- Approval, scheduling, sending, archive, and history foundations.
- Attachments.
- Employee and agent landing-page announcements.
- Announcement conversations and moderation foundations.
- SMTP and Microsoft Graph delivery integration.
- Test and live email-governance modes.

### Current work in progress

The delivery-reliability batch covers duplicate-send protection, browser-timeout resilience, server-tracked asynchronous delivery, strict separation of test and live recipients, and expanded history. This batch must remain marked **in progress** until validated.

### Styling boundary

Email templates use Outlook-compatible ATD branding. Portal Glassmorphism styling must not be applied to email.

## 8. Access management

### Functional scope

- Access requests.
- Role and permission management.
- Temporary or governed entitlements.
- Granted-access display.
- Support groups.
- Administrative review and audit evidence.

Functions already available to a requester should be greyed out. Granted access should be shown separately with its source, such as default, permission, or role. Governed revocation must remain available.

## 9. Dashboards and customisation

The application contains employee and operational dashboards, templates, widgets, priority queues, announcements, and configurable appearance foundations.

Planned appearance capabilities include:

- Light and Dark modes.
- Glassmorphism as an optional portal styling theme.
- Named custom layout templates.
- Responsive layouts and accessible contrast.
- Reduced-transparency or no-blur fallback.

## 10. Knowledge, catalogue, assets, and workspaces

The source contains foundations for knowledge content, request catalogue, guided routing, asset/configuration records, and department or operational workspaces. The final documentation will map each user-facing route and management workflow in later groups.

## 11. Reporting and audit

The system contains audit, activity, health, SLA, statistics, and reporting foundations. Weekly operational reporting is expected to cover assignment volumes, response and resolution durations, SLA performance, request patterns, portal usage, recurring terms, and agent/manager access.

## 12. Integrations

- Microsoft Graph for email and Microsoft-related functions.
- SMTP delivery.
- Microsoft directory synchronisation.
- Inbound email-to-ticket.
- Freshservice historical archive/import functions.
- Production reporting and synchronisation services.

## 13. Capability verification checklist

Before a capability is marked deployed:

1. Confirm active source and migration state.
2. Confirm frontend build and backend checks.
3. Validate role and permission behaviour.
4. Validate negative and unauthorised paths.
5. Validate audit and notification evidence.
6. Confirm deployment commit and production state.
7. Update the capability register and development tracker.

# ATD Helpdesk Technical Architecture

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source branch:** `feature/launch-communications-queue-compatibility`
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-1-SCRIPT-586`

## 1. Architecture summary

ATD Helpdesk is a browser-based service-management platform with a React/Vite frontend, Node.js/Express backend, PostgreSQL application database, and selected external integration services.

```text
Browser
  |
  | HTTPS / REST
  v
React + Vite frontend
  |
  | /api
  v
Node.js + Express backend
  |
  +--> PostgreSQL application database
  +--> Microsoft Graph and SMTP email
  +--> Microsoft directory services
  +--> Inbound email processing
  +--> Microsoft SQL Server production-data integration
  +--> Freshservice historical integration
```

## 2. Frontend layer

### Responsibilities

- Route and page composition.
- Role-aware navigation.
- Forms, filters, dialogs, tables, cards, queues, workspaces, and previews.
- User feedback, notifications, and interaction state.
- API consumption through shared service clients.
- Portal appearance and responsive behaviour.

### Architecture rules

- Do not treat hidden frontend controls as authorisation.
- Preserve filters, drafts, selections, and scroll position during live workspace updates where supported.
- Standardise Back to Admin and Back to Dashboard controls.
- Apply Glassmorphism only through portal theme/layout styling.
- Maintain accessible contrast and fallback behaviour.

## 3. Backend layer

### Responsibilities

- Authentication and current-user context.
- Backend authorisation and governed actions.
- Ticket, user, notification, communications, archive, knowledge, catalogue, and administration APIs.
- Validation and business rules.
- Audit creation.
- Email and external integration orchestration.
- Background scheduling and recurring processing.

### API model

The backend exposes REST APIs under `/api`. Route modules should remain thin where possible, delegating business logic to services. High-impact operations require explicit validation, permissions, and audit evidence.

## 4. Data layer

### PostgreSQL

PostgreSQL is the primary application data store. Repository migrations define and evolve the application schema. Documentation of individual tables, keys, indexes, and migration order will be produced in the database documentation group.

### Microsoft SQL Server

A separate Microsoft SQL Server integration layer supports production-related data or reporting. This external store must not be treated as the authoritative application database unless an integration contract explicitly states otherwise.

### Data-management rules

- Migrations must be ordered, reviewable, and reversible where practical.
- Existing data must be preserved during rebranding and capability repair.
- Production schema state must be verified rather than inferred from local migrations.
- High-impact deletion requires dependency checks and durable evidence.

## 5. Background processing

The source contains scheduler or background-processing foundations for reminders, inbound email, Company Communications, and production synchronisation.

Background jobs must provide:

- Idempotent operation.
- Durable states.
- Retry and failure evidence.
- Protection against duplicate delivery.
- Timestamps and actor or system attribution.
- Safe start-up and restart behaviour.

The Company Communications asynchronous delivery conversion remains in progress.

## 6. Email architecture

Email can be delivered through SMTP or Microsoft Graph. Shared email governance controls determine Disabled, Testing, or Live behaviour. Environment-level redirect configuration must be visible in evidence because an environment override can supersede the intended governance mode.

Test delivery and live audience delivery must remain isolated. No documentation or validation step should send live email without explicit governed approval.

## 7. Authentication and authorisation

Authentication establishes the current user. Authorisation combines roles, permissions, defaults, and governed grants. The backend must validate every protected action irrespective of frontend visibility.

Administrative and high-impact functions require stronger controls, including confirmation, reason capture, typed confirmation where appropriate, audit evidence, and restricted roles.

## 8. File and media handling

The system contains profile media, ticket attachments, communication attachments, downloads, and preview foundations. File access must remain authorised, path-safe, and content-type aware. Uploaded operational content is excluded from source-collection reports.

## 9. Observability and audit

Operational evidence includes:

- Application logs.
- Health endpoints or health evidence.
- Audit records.
- Ticket activity.
- Notification records.
- Delivery history.
- Deployment and validation reports.

A successful request is not sufficient evidence for high-impact processing. Durable database state and audit evidence should be used where available.

## 10. Deployment boundary

Deployment is a separate governed process involving validation, curated Git changes, remote backup, migration review, dependency handling, frontend activation, process restart, and production verification.

Documentation generation does not build, deploy, migrate, email, stage, commit, or connect to production.

## 11. Source precedence

When sources conflict, use this order:

1. Current `backend/src` and `frontend/src`.
2. Current database migrations.
3. Current configuration and package metadata.
4. Verified deployment or production evidence.
5. Current trackers and capability register.
6. Current README and documentation.
7. Release packages.
8. Backups and historical source.

## 12. Planned architecture documentation

After deployment, the documentation programme will add:

- Modular architecture reference.
- Architecture Decision Record library.
- Function rationale records.
- Failure-mode register.
- Decision playbooks.
- Complete route and function reference.


END OF FILE: 03_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Roles, Access and Governance

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-1-SCRIPT-586`

## 1. Governance purpose

Access governance ensures that users receive only the functions required for authorised work, while preserving traceability, revocation, separation of duties, and protection of high-impact actions.

## 2. User audiences

### Employee

Typical scope includes personal requests, permitted catalogue and knowledge functions, personal notifications, announcements, profile settings, and authorised employee dashboards.

### Agent

Typical scope includes support queues, assigned work, ticket investigation, communication, internal notes, status updates, attachments, activity, and operational notifications.

### Manager

Typical scope includes team or department visibility, queue oversight, approvals, performance information, escalations, reporting, and selected governed controls.

### Administrator

Typical scope includes user administration, roles, permissions, support groups, access requests, email governance, communications, configuration, audit, and health evidence.

### Superadministrator

Typical scope includes restricted high-impact actions such as governed permanent deletion and other explicitly protected controls. Superadministrator access must not remove the need for evidence, reason capture, confirmation, and audit.

The exact effective permission set must always be derived from current backend rules, not from these audience summaries.

## 3. Sources of access

Effective access may be provided by:

- Default access.
- Direct permission.
- Role membership.
- Temporary or governed entitlement.
- Support-group or organisational context where explicitly implemented.

The access-request interface must grey out functions already available. Granted access must be displayed separately with its source. All governed grants must remain revocable through authorised controls.

## 4. Enforcement model

### Backend enforcement

Every protected API action must validate the current user, required role or permission, applicable scope, and any additional governance condition.

### Frontend enforcement

The frontend should hide, disable, or explain unavailable actions, but frontend behaviour is not a security boundary.

### Scope enforcement

Access may be limited by requester ownership, assignment, support group, department, management scope, or administrative authority. Exact scope rules will be mapped in later route-level documentation.

## 5. Access-request workflow

A governed access request should record:

1. Requester.
2. Requested function or entitlement.
3. Business reason.
4. Current access and grant source.
5. Approval or rejection actor.
6. Decision timestamp.
7. Effective and expiry dates where applicable.
8. Revocation evidence.
9. Notifications and audit trail.

Pending actions must remain actionable after their notification is marked read.

## 6. High-impact actions

High-impact actions require proportionate safeguards. Examples include:

- Permanent ticket deletion.
- Bulk archive or delete.
- Role or permission changes.
- Live whole-company communications.
- Email-governance changes.
- Directory changes.
- Production configuration changes.

Safeguards may include explicit approval, typed confirmation, reason capture, dependency checks, preview, restricted role, test mode, and durable audit evidence.

## 7. Ticket governance

- Requester-visible replies and internal notes must remain clearly distinct.
- Ticket activity must record participant, action, and timestamp.
- Reopen actions must be explicit and reportable.
- Bulk operations must provide governed confirmation.
- Archive, restore, and permanent deletion must remain separate lifecycle actions.
- Assignee and support-group changes must be auditable.

## 8. Communications governance

- Test recipients and live audiences must be isolated.
- Whole-company, department, management, position, exclusions, and external recipients must be resolved transparently.
- Test or development validation must not contact live audiences.
- Delivery status, intended recipient, provider, timestamp, and failure evidence should be retained.
- Environment email redirects must be visible in operational evidence.

## 9. Directory governance

Microsoft Directory synchronisation must not perform writes when an account has no changes. Actual account changes should be consolidated into Monday 08:00 weekly alerts with actor details and attached to the governed weekly reporting process.

## 10. Audit requirements

Significant activity should record:

- Actor or system identity.
- Action.
- Target entity.
- Before and after state where appropriate.
- Timestamp.
- Reason or approval evidence.
- Result or failure.
- Related ticket, campaign, request, or user reference.

## 11. Segregation and least privilege

- Default to minimum necessary access.
- Separate request, approval, and high-impact execution where practical.
- Do not infer authorisation from job title alone.
- Keep temporary access time-bound.
- Revoke obsolete access through governed controls.
- Review privileged access and role definitions regularly.

## 12. Governance validation checklist

Before releasing a governed function:

1. Test an authorised user.
2. Test an unauthorised user.
3. Test an out-of-scope user.
4. Confirm backend rejection.
5. Confirm frontend explanation.
6. Confirm audit evidence.
7. Confirm notifications.
8. Confirm rollback or recovery behaviour.
9. Confirm no unintended email or directory write.
10. Update trackers and documentation.


END OF FILE: 04_ROLES_ACCESS_AND_GOVERNANCE.md

FILE: 05_TICKET_LIFECYCLE_AND_WORKFLOWS.md

# ATD Helpdesk Ticket Lifecycle and Workflows

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Status:** Ticketing and operations reference
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-2-SCRIPT-587`

## 1. Purpose

This document describes the governed lifecycle of a ticket in ATD Helpdesk, from creation to closure, reopening, archive, restoration, and deletion. Exact frontend visibility depends on the current user's role, permission, ownership, department, support group, and assignment scope.

## 2. Ticket categories

ATD Helpdesk supports operational records representing incidents and service requests. A ticket should preserve its type and related service information so that queues, reporting, routing, SLA rules, and management statistics can distinguish the work accurately.

Required filtering dimensions include:

- Department
- Requestor
- Priority
- Incident type
- Service-request type
- Support group
- Assignee
- Status and lifecycle state
- SLA state

## 3. Creation workflow

A ticket creation workflow should capture the minimum information required to understand and route the request:

1. Requestor identity and contact context.
2. Department or organisational context.
3. Ticket type.
4. Subject and description.
5. Priority or impact information.
6. Service category or request type.
7. Attachments where necessary.
8. Optional collaborators or employee CC recipients where enabled.
9. The source of the request, such as portal, email, integration, or authorised administration.

After creation, the system should assign a durable ticket reference, record the creation timestamp, create the opening activity, and notify authorised participants according to configured notification rules.

## 4. Lifecycle states

The exact database values remain authoritative. The functional lifecycle includes these conceptual states:

- **New or Open:** The request exists and awaits triage or work.
- **Assigned:** An agent or support group is responsible for the request.
- **In progress:** Investigation or fulfilment is underway.
- **Pending:** Work is waiting for requester input, approval, dependency, supplier, scheduled activity, or another governed condition.
- **Resolved:** A solution has been supplied and the ticket may still be reopened.
- **Closed:** The operational lifecycle is complete according to closure rules.
- **Reopened:** A previously resolved issue has reappeared or requires further work.
- **Archived:** The ticket is removed from active workspaces but retained.
- **Pending deletion:** The governed retention countdown is active.
- **Permanently deleted:** A restricted deletion action has completed and durable evidence is retained.

A status change must record the actor, previous state, new state, timestamp, and reason where required.

## 5. Triage and classification

Triage establishes the correct priority, type, support group, assignee, service category, and SLA basis. Agents should be able to identify insufficient information and request clarification without losing the original request context.

Triage changes must be reflected in ticket activity and relevant notifications. Changes that affect SLA handling, responsibility, or requester expectations require clear evidence.

## 6. Assignment workflow

Assignment may target an agent, support group, or other authorised operational owner. Where assignment acceptance is enabled, assignment and acceptance are distinct events:

1. An authorised actor assigns the ticket.
2. The selected agent receives an actionable notification.
3. The agent accepts or rejects according to governed rules.
4. The acceptance decision is recorded with actor and timestamp.
5. The ticket header and activity show the effective assignee.

A rejected or expired assignment must not silently leave a ticket without operational ownership.

## 7. Conversation and collaboration

Ticket communication should preserve a complete chronological conversation with timestamps and participants.

### Requester-visible reply

A requester-visible reply is part of the external ticket conversation and may trigger email or portal notification to the requester and authorised participants.

### Internal note

An internal note is restricted to authorised support users. Internal notes must never be exposed to the requester through the portal, outbound email, exports, or unauthorised APIs.

### Participants

The platform roadmap includes optional collaborators, an employee CC picker, optional support-group alerts, assignee email, and Teams ticket-chat synchronisation. These capabilities must be marked according to verified implementation status before being presented as complete.

## 8. Attachments

Attachments should retain:

- Uploader
- Upload timestamp
- Original filename
- Stored reference
- Content type and size
- Ticket relationship
- Authorisation requirements

Supported files may be previewed inline or in a full-screen viewer. Download and preview endpoints must enforce ticket access.

## 9. Resolution and closure

Resolution should record the solution, resolver, resolution timestamp, relevant categorisation, and any requester communication. Closure should follow configured governance and should preserve the complete audit and conversation history.

Resolved and closed surveys are compulsory roadmap capabilities. Documentation must not classify surveys as deployed without source and validation evidence.

## 10. Reopening

Employees must be able to reopen a resolved ticket when the issue reappears, subject to the configured eligibility rules. Reopening must:

- Create a distinct activity entry.
- Identify the actor and timestamp.
- Preserve the previous resolution.
- Return the ticket to an actionable state.
- Notify the appropriate operational owner.
- Increment reopened-ticket statistics.
- Support repeat-issue indicators in management reporting.

## 11. Bulk operations

Governed bulk operations include selection and authorised changes such as edit, assignment, archive, and deletion. Bulk actions must:

- Work in the ticket workspace and Dashboard Ticket Priority queue where supported.
- Show the selected ticket count.
- Preview the intended action.
- Require confirmation for high-impact changes.
- Apply permission and scope checks to every ticket.
- Record per-ticket success and failure.
- Preserve rollback or recovery evidence where practical.
- Offer the required Microsoft Teams prompt where configured.

## 12. Failure and recovery principles

- A failed action must not report success.
- Repeated requests must not create duplicate activity or communication.
- Partial bulk operations must report each outcome.
- Ticket drafts, filters, selection, and scroll position should be preserved during safe refresh where supported.
- Repairs must preserve existing ticket data and working functionality.

## 13. Validation checklist

1. Create each supported ticket type.
2. Validate required fields and unauthorised paths.
3. Validate classification, assignment, acceptance, and reassignment.
4. Validate requester replies and internal-note isolation.
5. Validate attachments and access restrictions.
6. Validate all lifecycle transitions.
7. Validate resolution, closure, and reopening.
8. Validate activity, notifications, timestamps, and actor evidence.
9. Validate bulk operations and partial failure reporting.
10. Validate reporting counts for reopened and repeat issues.


END OF FILE: 05_TICKET_LIFECYCLE_AND_WORKFLOWS.md

SIZE: 5524 bytes

# ATD Helpdesk Assignment, Routing and SLA

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-2-SCRIPT-587`

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the functional expectations for ticket routing, assignment, agent availability, escalation, and service-level tracking.

## 2. Routing inputs

Routing decisions may use:

- Ticket type
- Service category
- Department
- Requestor context
- Priority and impact
- Support group
- Agent skills or permissions
- Agent availability
- Existing workload
- Catalogue or guided-request rules
- Email-to-ticket attributes
- Manual governed assignment

The backend remains authoritative for the resolved support group and assignee.

## 3. Support-group routing

A support group represents an authorised operational team. Routing should:

1. Resolve the correct group from explicit selection or configured rules.
2. Verify that the group is active and authorised for the function.
3. Notify the appropriate group or member according to configuration.
4. Record routing evidence in ticket activity.
5. Preserve the previous owner when a change occurs.
6. Avoid silently routing to an unavailable or invalid destination.

Optional support-group alerts remain subject to implementation verification.

## 4. Agent assignment

An assignment should identify:

- Assigning actor
- Assigned agent
- Support group
- Assignment timestamp
- Acceptance status where enabled
- Acceptance or rejection timestamp
- Reason for governed reassignment where required

The ticket header should expose the effective assignee, creation date, and deadline or SLA date to authorised users.

## 5. Assignment acceptance

An actionable assignment notification should remain actionable even when marked read. The acceptance workflow must revalidate the current ticket and user state before applying the action.

A stale acceptance attempt should return a clear result rather than overwrite a later assignment.

## 6. Agent availability

Agent availability is a compulsory roadmap capability. Expected states may include available, busy, unavailable, off duty, or another approved operational vocabulary. Routing must not rely on an availability value until the capability is fully implemented and governed.

Availability changes should record actor, effective time, and optional expiry. Managers should have authorised visibility into team availability.

## 7. Priority

Priority determines urgency and may influence response targets, queue order, notification, and escalation. Priority changes require appropriate authorisation and activity evidence.

Dashboard priority queues must support broad filtering and governed bulk operations without bypassing ticket-level permissions.

## 8. SLA definitions

An SLA can include initial-response, update, resolution, or other service targets. The applicable SLA must be selected from the ticket's operational context and retained for reporting.

### Syspro/ERP initial-response targets

- **Critical:** 30 minutes
- **High:** 3 hours
- **Medium:** 24 hours
- **Low:** 48 hours

These values are initial-response targets, not resolution guarantees.

### Infrastructure SLA

Infrastructure retains a separate, faster SLA matrix. Infrastructure targets must not be replaced by the Syspro/ERP values.

## 9. SLA clock behaviour

The exact implementation and business calendar are authoritative. SLA documentation must identify:

- Start event
- Target timestamp
- Time zone
- Business hours or calendar basis
- Pause conditions
- Resume conditions
- First-response evidence
- Breach state
- Resolution evidence
- Reopen behaviour

A ticket reopened after resolution must not erase the original SLA evidence. Reporting should distinguish the initial lifecycle from reopened work.

## 10. Escalation

Escalation may be triggered by urgency, approaching breach, breach, reassignment failure, prolonged pending state, or a manager decision. Escalation should produce a durable activity record and appropriate notification.

An escalation must not silently change ownership or priority without recording the change.

## 11. Notifications

Routing and SLA notification settings must accommodate assignment, acceptance, approaching breach, breach, status change, requester reply, approval, and other configured events.

Pending actions must remain available after a notification is marked read.

## 12. Reporting

Operational reporting should support:

- Assignment volume
- Unassigned tickets
- Acceptance duration
- Agent and support-group workload
- Initial-response duration
- Resolution duration
- SLA compliance
- Breach reasons
- Reassignment frequency
- Reopened incidents
- Repeat-issue indicators

## 13. Validation checklist

1. Validate automatic and manual support-group routing.
2. Validate assignment, acceptance, rejection, and reassignment.
3. Validate stale or conflicting actions.
4. Validate agent availability when implemented.
5. Validate each priority and SLA matrix.
6. Validate SLA start, pause, resume, response, breach, and resolution evidence.
7. Validate dashboards, filters, queues, and bulk operations.
8. Validate notifications and escalation.
9. Validate unauthorised and out-of-scope users.
10. Reconcile reporting totals with ticket history.


END OF FILE: 06_ASSIGNMENT_ROUTING_AND_SLA.md

SIZE: 5439 bytes

# ATD Helpdesk Ticket Activity, Notifications and Audit

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-2-SCRIPT-587`

## 1. Purpose

Ticket activity, notifications, and audit evidence provide related but distinct records of system use.

- **Ticket activity** explains what happened on a ticket.
- **Notifications** tell a user that something requires attention or has changed.
- **Audit evidence** provides durable governance and investigation records.

## 2. Ticket activity requirements

Each significant ticket event should identify:

- Participant or system actor
- Activity type
- Human-readable summary
- Ticket reference
- Timestamp
- Previous and new values where appropriate
- Related comment, attachment, assignment, approval, or lifecycle event
- Visibility classification where appropriate

Examples include creation, classification, priority change, assignment, acceptance, reply, internal note, attachment, status change, resolution, closure, reopening, archive, restore, and deletion action.

## 3. Compact activity display

The ticket detail interface may show a compact latest-activity view with a small number of recent entries and a View more or View less control. Compact presentation must not delete or conceal the durable history from authorised users.

## 4. Notification Centre

The Notification Centre supports persistent records and user-facing organisation. Confirmed foundations include:

- All, Approvals, and Discussions filtering
- Read and unread state
- Mark all as read
- Action-required notifications
- Actor avatar or initials
- Type, reference, summary, and timestamp
- Protection of pending actions from Clear completed

## 5. Actionable notifications

Marking a notification as read acknowledges visibility only. It must not complete, reject, remove, or disable a pending action.

Before applying an action from a notification, the backend must revalidate:

1. Current user authorisation.
2. Current entity state.
3. Current ownership or scope.
4. Whether the action is still pending.
5. Whether a newer action superseded the notification.

## 6. Required notification events

Notification settings are expected to cover detailed events including:

- Approvals
- Ticket updates
- Assignment and acceptance
- Solution articles
- Service categories
- Task discussions
- Journey request and task events
- Requester replies
- Internal operational events where authorised
- SLA and escalation events
- Archive, restore, and governed deletion events

Each event must be classified against current implementation evidence before publication as complete.

## 7. Floating change notifications

A compact centred floating notification should describe a completed amendment and provide an Undo option where safe and supported.

Undo must:

- Be time-bound where appropriate.
- Revalidate the current state.
- Refuse unsafe reversal.
- Create its own activity and audit evidence.
- Avoid reversing irreversible external side effects such as a delivered email.

Expanded notification history and a separate detailed Activity view may be implemented later.

## 8. Audit evidence

Audit entries for governed actions should include:

- Actor or system identity
- Action name
- Target entity and identifier
- Timestamp
- Request or correlation identifier where available
- Before and after state where appropriate
- Reason, approval, or confirmation evidence
- Result and failure information
- Source channel

Audit evidence should be immutable to ordinary users and accessible only within authorised scope.

## 9. Privacy and visibility

- Internal notes must not appear in requester notifications.
- Personal information must be limited to authorised operational need.
- Notification text should avoid exposing restricted ticket content on shared displays.
- Audit access must be permission controlled.
- Attachment links must enforce current authorisation when opened.

## 10. Delivery and retry

Notification creation and external message delivery are separate outcomes. A notification may exist even when an email is skipped or fails. The system should preserve provider, attempt time, failure, intended recipient, and governance evidence where applicable.

Duplicate processing must not create duplicate durable notifications or repeat an irreversible external action.

## 11. Retention and clearing

User-facing Clear completed behaviour should remove or hide only eligible completed notifications. It must not remove unresolved approvals, assignments, access requests, or other pending actions.

Audit and ticket activity retention is governed separately from personal notification presentation.

## 12. Validation checklist

1. Generate every supported ticket activity type.
2. Verify actor, activity, and timestamp.
3. Verify requester and internal visibility boundaries.
4. Verify read and unread controls.
5. Verify pending actions remain actionable after read.
6. Verify stale actions fail safely.
7. Verify Clear completed protection.
8. Verify floating notifications and safe Undo where implemented.
9. Verify duplicate-processing safeguards.
10. Verify audit access, retention, and export boundaries.


END OF FILE: 07_TICKET_ACTIVITY_NOTIFICATIONS_AND_AUDIT.md

# ATD Helpdesk Archive, Reopen and Retention

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-2-SCRIPT-587`

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the functional boundaries between active tickets, resolved or closed tickets, reopened tickets, archived tickets, pending deletion, permanent deletion, and historical external-system records.

## 2. Current-ticket lifecycle areas

The current-ticket archive experience includes conceptual areas for:

- Active Tickets
- Archived Tickets
- Pending Deletion

Archiving removes a ticket from normal active workspaces without immediately deleting its data. Restoration returns an eligible archived or pending-deletion ticket to an authorised lifecycle state.

## 3. Archive action

An archive action should record:

- Ticket reference
- Requester
- Department
- Previous status
- Archive actor
- Archive timestamp
- Archive reason
- Applicable retention or deletion date

Archive permission and eligibility must be checked by the backend. A ticket with blocking dependencies should not be silently archived.

## 4. Pending deletion

The implemented lifecycle foundation uses a seven-day governance countdown for eligible current tickets. The production configuration and actual timestamp remain authoritative.

During pending deletion:

- The ticket remains recoverable through authorised restoration.
- The deletion date is visible to authorised users.
- Dependencies and current state remain subject to validation.
- Ordinary users cannot permanently delete the record.

## 5. Permanent deletion

Permanent deletion is restricted to the authorised Superadministrator function and requires high-impact safeguards, including:

- Permission and role validation
- Reason capture
- Typed confirmation
- Dependency checks
- Current-state revalidation
- Durable tombstone or deletion evidence
- Actor and timestamp
- Clear success or failure result

Permanent deletion must not be confused with hiding, archiving, or removing a ticket from a personal view.

## 6. Restoration

A restoration action should:

1. Revalidate the current lifecycle state.
2. Confirm that the ticket has not already been deleted.
3. Restore the correct prior or governed active state.
4. Preserve archive and restoration history.
5. Record actor, reason, and timestamp.
6. Notify authorised stakeholders where configured.

## 7. Reopening resolved tickets

Reopening is an operational lifecycle action, not an archive restoration action.

When a resolved issue reappears, an eligible employee should be able to reopen the ticket. The system must preserve the previous resolution and record the reopening event. Reopened incidents must be explicitly represented in statistics and management reporting.

## 8. Repeat-issue indicators

Management reporting should distinguish:

- First occurrence
- Reopened occurrence
- New ticket related to a previous issue
- Recurring category or service pattern

A repeat-issue indicator should be evidence based and should not automatically merge unrelated tickets.

## 9. Freshservice historical archive

Freshservice archive, import, or merge functionality is a historical external-system capability. It is separate from the lifecycle of current ATD Helpdesk tickets.

Documentation and user interfaces must not imply that deleting or restoring a current ticket changes the retained Freshservice historical source unless an explicit governed integration provides that behaviour.

## 10. Attachments and dependent records

Archive and retention must account for:

- Attachments
- Comments and internal notes
- Activity and audit entries
- Participants
- Notifications
- Assignment records
- Approval records
- Survey records
- External references
- Reporting relationships

Dependency checks should prevent incomplete deletion or preserve required tombstone evidence.

## 11. Retention policy

Final retention periods must be approved by organisational governance and recorded in configuration or policy documentation. The application should distinguish operational lifecycle dates from legal, audit, security, or backup retention requirements.

## 12. Reporting

Archive and retention reporting should support:

- Archived ticket count
- Archive actor and reason
- Pending-deletion count
- Upcoming deletion dates
- Restored ticket count
- Permanent deletion evidence
- Reopened count
- Repeat-issue indicators
- Department, requester, priority, type, and support-group filtering

## 13. Validation checklist

1. Archive an eligible ticket.
2. Reject an unauthorised archive.
3. Verify archive fields and activity.
4. Verify the seven-day pending-deletion presentation.
5. Restore from archived and pending-deletion states.
6. Test dependency-blocked deletion.
7. Test reason and typed confirmation.
8. Verify Superadministrator-only permanent deletion.
9. Verify tombstone or deletion evidence.
10. Reopen a resolved ticket and reconcile statistics.
11. Confirm Freshservice history remains separate.
12. Verify attachments and dependent-record handling.


END OF FILE: 08_ARCHIVE_REOPEN_AND_RETENTION.md

# ATD Helpdesk Employee Experience

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Status:** Employee experience reference
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-3-SCRIPT-588`

## 1. Purpose

This guide describes the employee-facing ATD Helpdesk experience. Actual visibility depends on the current user's account status, department, default access, roles, direct permissions, and governed entitlements.

## 2. Signing in and first access

Employee access may include account registration, approval, authentication, terms acceptance, and first-login onboarding. The system should provide clear guidance when an account is awaiting approval, missing required acceptance, or not authorised for a requested function.

Employees should not be shown administrative actions merely because a frontend route is known. The backend remains authoritative for access.

## 3. Employee landing page

The employee landing experience should provide a concise view of authorised functions, including:

- Create a request or ticket.
- Review personal requests.
- View relevant announcements.
- Open notifications and pending actions.
- Access permitted knowledge and catalogue functions.
- Use pinned or configured dashboard actions.
- Review profile and appearance settings.

Company announcements may target the whole company, selected departments, management levels, combined position levels, exclusions, or authorised external recipients. Only announcements matching the current employee audience should appear.

## 4. Creating a request

An employee should select the appropriate incident or service-request path and supply enough information for effective routing. The request experience may include:

- Subject and description.
- Service category or request type.
- Priority or impact information where permitted.
- Department and requestor context.
- Attachments.
- Optional collaborators or employee CC recipients when implemented.

After submission, the employee should receive a durable reference and be able to review the request status.

## 5. Tracking requests

Employees should be able to review authorised ticket information, including:

- Ticket reference and subject.
- Creation date.
- Current status.
- Effective assignee or support group where permitted.
- Deadline or SLA information where appropriate.
- Requester-visible conversation.
- Attachments available to the requester.
- Resolution and closure information.

Internal notes, restricted audit evidence, and administrative metadata must not be exposed.

## 6. Replies and attachments

Employee replies become part of the requester-visible conversation and should retain participant and timestamp information. Attachments must be authorised, path safe, and associated with the correct ticket.

Supported attachments may be previewed inline or in full-screen view where implemented. Download access must be revalidated when the file is opened.

## 7. Resolution, surveys, and reopening

When a ticket is resolved, the employee should receive the applicable solution and status update. Resolved and closed surveys are planned compulsory capabilities and must not be described as deployed until validated.

If the issue reappears, an eligible employee must be able to reopen the resolved ticket. Reopening must create a distinct activity record and preserve the earlier resolution.

## 8. Notifications

Employees may receive notifications for request updates, replies, approvals, assignments relevant to the employee, solution content, service categories, task discussions, and journey events where implemented.

Marking a notification as read must not complete or remove a pending action. Pending actions must remain available until completed, rejected, expired, or superseded through governed processing.

## 9. Company announcements

Authorised announcements may appear on the employee landing page. An announcement may include content, attachments, publication information, and an optional governed conversation.

Portal announcement presentation follows portal themes. Email copies use independent Outlook-compatible ATD branding and do not use Glassmorphism.

## 10. Profile and avatar

Employees should be able to review and update supported profile information. The current profile image should load consistently across authorised portal surfaces, refresh after change, avoid stale cached images, and provide a suitable fallback.

Profile media endpoints must enforce current-user and authorised administrative access.

## 11. Appearance and layout

Employee appearance settings may include:

- Light and Dark modes.
- Colour or appearance themes.
- Glassmorphism as an optional portal theme and layout style.
- Named custom dashboard-layout templates.
- Responsive dashboard arrangements.
- Reduced-transparency or no-blur fallbacks.

Glassmorphism applies only to portal styling. Glassmorphism does not apply to emails.

## 12. Access requests

Employees may request additional functions through the governed access-request workflow. Functions already available should be greyed out. Granted access should appear in a separate view with the grant source, such as default, permission, or role.

The request should include a business reason and should remain traceable through approval, rejection, expiry, or revocation.

## 13. Knowledge and service catalogue

The employee experience may expose authorised knowledge content, solution articles, service categories, catalogue items, and guided request paths. The system should direct employees toward an appropriate request type without blocking legitimate ticket creation when assistance is still required.

Production line reports are limited to active Network catalogue items in IT or active line-issue items in PDM. These tickets are routed automatically to the appropriate support group. A Production operator may report a PDM issue for a supervisor only when the supervisor cannot access their email account, and the reason must be recorded. Finance ticket requesters may link an active employee KPI or skip KPI linking and provide an issue summary; linked KPIs remain visible in the Finance ticket register.

## 14. Privacy and safe use

Employees should:

- Avoid placing passwords or secrets in tickets.
- Include only the necessary personal or business information.
- Use requester replies for communication intended for support and authorised participants.
- Review attachment contents before upload.
- Report incorrect access or unintended information exposure.

## 15. Employee validation checklist

1. Sign in and complete first-access requirements.
2. Review the employee landing page.
3. Create each authorised request type.
4. Track, reply to, and attach files to a request.
5. Confirm internal information is not exposed.
6. Review notifications and pending actions.
7. Review announcements and attachments.
8. Update profile and avatar.
9. Change supported appearance settings.
10. Submit and track an access request.
11. Resolve and reopen an eligible ticket.
12. Validate responsive behaviour on supported screen sizes.


END OF FILE: 09_EMPLOYEE_EXPERIENCE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Agent, Manager and Operations Guide

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-3-SCRIPT-588`

## 1. Purpose

This guide describes operational use by agents and managers. Exact capabilities depend on role, permission, department, support group, assignment, and management scope.

## 2. Operational landing experience

Authorised operational users may access:

- Ticket workspaces and queues.
- Priority views.
- Assigned and group work.
- Ticket detail and activity.
- Notifications and pending actions.
- Company announcements.
- Dashboard templates and widgets.
- Reporting or statistics appropriate to scope.

The interface should preserve working context during safe refresh, including filters, drafts, selections, and scroll position where supported.

## 3. Queue management

Operational queues should support broad filtering by:

- Department.
- Requestor.
- Priority.
- Incident type.
- Service-request type.
- Support group.
- Assignee.
- Status.
- SLA state.

Bulk ticket operations must also work within the Dashboard Ticket Priority queue and must apply ticket-level permission checks.

## 4. Ticket triage

An agent should validate the request, classification, priority, support group, assignee, service category, and SLA basis. Any change must be recorded with actor and timestamp.

Agents should request missing information through a requester-visible reply and must use internal notes only for authorised operational discussion.

## 5. Assignment and acceptance

Assignment may target an agent or support group. Where acceptance is enabled:

1. The assignment creates an actionable notification.
2. The assigned agent reviews the current ticket.
3. The backend revalidates the assignment before acceptance.
4. Acceptance or rejection is recorded.
5. The ticket displays the effective assignee.

A notification remains actionable after being marked read until the underlying action is completed or no longer valid.

## 6. Working a ticket

During investigation or fulfilment, agents should:

- Record meaningful progress.
- Distinguish requester replies from internal notes.
- Preserve complete timestamps and participants.
- Attach only relevant and authorised files.
- Update priority, status, assignment, and due information through governed controls.
- Avoid exposing restricted operational information to the requester.
- Record a clear solution before resolution.

## 7. Manager oversight

Managers may have authorised visibility into team or department queues, workloads, SLA risk, reassignment, escalations, reopened issues, repeat-issue indicators, and operational reporting.

Management access must remain scoped. A management title alone must not grant unrestricted system administration.

## 8. Agent availability and workload

Agent availability is a compulsory roadmap capability. Until validated, availability must not be presented as a reliable routing control.

Expected operational views may include:

- Available or unavailable agents.
- Assigned workload.
- Unaccepted assignments.
- Priority distribution.
- Approaching SLA breach.
- Reassignment frequency.
- Reopened work.

## 9. SLA and escalation

Agents and managers should understand whether displayed targets are initial-response or resolution targets. Syspro/ERP initial-response targets are:

- Critical: 30 minutes.
- High: 3 hours.
- Medium: 24 hours.
- Low: 48 hours.

Infrastructure retains a separate faster SLA matrix. Escalation decisions and SLA-affecting changes must be recorded.

## 10. Notifications and change confirmation

Operational notifications may cover assignment, approval, requester replies, ticket changes, discussions, relevant catalogue events, and SLA conditions.

Brief floating notifications should describe recorded changes and offer Undo only where reversal is safe. Detailed change confirmations should show what changed and what evidence was recorded.

## 11. Archive and reopen operations

Agents and managers may archive, restore, or reopen tickets only within authorised rules. Permanent deletion remains a restricted Superadministrator action.

Reopened incidents must remain visible in statistics and management reporting. Reopening is distinct from restoring an archived ticket.

## 12. Attachments and previews

Agents may preview and download authorised attachments. Full-screen viewing should preserve access checks. Unsupported content should be offered through a safe download path rather than unsafe inline rendering.

## 13. Communications and announcements

Authorised operational users may see targeted announcements. Company Communications administration is separate from ordinary agent work unless permission is explicitly granted.

Live company email must not be tested casually. Testing mode and explicit test recipients must remain isolated from live audiences.

## 14. Reporting expectations

Operational reporting should support:

- Ticket volumes.
- Assignment and acceptance counts.
- First-response and resolution durations.
- SLA compliance and breaches.
- Reopened tickets.
- Repeat issues.
- Request patterns.
- Agent and support-group workload.
- Department, requestor, priority, and type filters.

## 15. Shift and handoff discipline

A handoff should identify:

- High-priority open tickets.
- Unaccepted assignments.
- Pending requester responses.
- Approaching or breached SLA targets.
- Escalations.
- Scheduled or dependent work.
- Relevant outages or system limitations.

The ticket record remains the authoritative operational history.

## 16. Operations validation checklist

1. Validate queue scope for each operational role.
2. Validate filters and bulk actions.
3. Validate triage and routing.
4. Validate assignment acceptance and stale actions.
5. Validate requester replies and internal-note isolation.
6. Validate attachments and previews.
7. Validate status, resolution, reopening, archive, and restore.
8. Validate SLA and escalation evidence.
9. Validate notifications, floating confirmations, and safe Undo.
10. Reconcile dashboards and reports with underlying ticket records.


END OF FILE: 10_AGENT_MANAGER_AND_OPERATIONS_GUIDE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Administration Guide

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-3-SCRIPT-588`

## 1. Purpose

This guide describes governed administrative responsibilities. Administrative visibility does not automatically authorise every action. The backend role and permission model remains authoritative.

## 2. Administrative areas

Administrative functions may include:

- User and account administration.
- Roles and permissions.
- Access requests and entitlements.
- Support groups.
- Microsoft Directory integration.
- Company Communications.
- Email governance.
- Request catalogue and knowledge administration.
- Asset or configuration records.
- Archive and retention controls.
- Notification settings.
- Audit and health evidence.
- Dashboard templates, themes, and layout management.

## 3. User administration

Authorised administrators may review account status, department, position, role, permissions, profile state, approval state, and directory evidence.

Administrative changes should:

1. Validate the current administrator.
2. Validate the target account and current state.
3. Show the proposed amendment.
4. Require a reason where appropriate.
5. Avoid writing when no value changes.
6. Record actor and timestamp.
7. Notify affected parties where governed.

## 4. Roles and permissions

Roles group permissions for defined responsibilities. Direct permissions should be used carefully because direct grants can be harder to review than role-based access.

Administrators should:

- Keep role names and purposes clear.
- Apply least privilege.
- Avoid duplicate or conflicting grants.
- Review inherited and direct access.
- Preserve revocation controls.
- Test backend enforcement.
- Record material changes.

## 5. Support groups

Support groups represent operational teams used for routing, assignment, alerts, and reporting. Group administration should preserve membership history, active state, routing purpose, and authorised management scope.

Removing a member must not silently orphan assigned work.

## 6. Microsoft Directory

Directory integration may synchronise account and organisational attributes. The integration must not write when an account has no actual changes.

Actual changes should be included in the Monday 08:00 weekly alert process with actor details and governed weekly-report attachment evidence.

Directory writes, if enabled, require explicit scope, failure handling, audit evidence, and protection against unintended bulk changes.

## 7. Company Communications administration

Authorised communication administrators may create, edit, approve, schedule, send, cancel, archive, and review communications according to governance.

Audience controls should support:

- Whole company.
- Department.
- Management level.
- Combined position level.
- Exclusions.
- Manually entered external recipients.

Testing and live delivery must remain separate. Glassmorphism is not an email-template style.

The asynchronous delivery-reliability batch remains in progress until separately validated.

## 8. Email governance

Email governance should expose the effective delivery mode:

- Disabled.
- Testing.
- Live.

Environment-level recipient redirection must be visible because an environment override may supersede the intended mode. Administrators must not switch live delivery without governed approval and test evidence.

## 9. Catalogue and knowledge administration

Administrative functions may manage service categories, catalogue items, guided request paths, and solution or knowledge content. Changes should preserve stable references where tickets, notifications, or reports depend on them.

## 10. Asset and configuration administration

Existing CMDB functionality and data must be preserved. The capability is to be rebranded throughout the portal after an approved replacement name is supplied. Rebranding may affect labels, navigation, routes where appropriate, permissions wording, dashboards, reports, notifications, and documentation.

Rebranding must not delete the underlying functionality or data.

## 11. Archive and retention administration

Administrators may govern archive rules and restoration according to permission. Permanent deletion is restricted to the authorised Superadministrator function and requires reason, typed confirmation, dependency checks, and durable evidence.

## 12. Notification settings

Notification administration should support detailed event selection, including approvals, ticket updates, assignments, solution articles, service categories, task discussions, and journey events.

Pending actions must remain actionable after read.

## 13. Dashboard, theme, and layout administration

Authorised administrative configuration may include dashboard templates, themes, Light/Dark modes, named custom layout templates, and portal styling options.

Glassmorphism is an optional portal theme/layout style. Administrators must maintain accessible contrast and fallback behaviour.

## 14. Audit and health

Administrators should use authorised audit and health views to investigate configuration, integration, processing, and access issues. Health evidence must avoid exposing secrets.

## 15. High-impact change procedure

Before a high-impact administrative change:

1. Identify the affected scope.
2. Capture the current state.
3. Validate permissions and separation of duties.
4. Preview the proposed result.
5. Create backup or rollback evidence where applicable.
6. Apply the minimum change.
7. Validate the result and negative paths.
8. Record actor, reason, timestamp, and outcome.
9. Notify affected parties where required.
10. Update documentation and trackers.

## 16. Administration validation checklist

1. Validate each administrative role and permission.
2. Validate user changes and no-change protection.
3. Validate support-group membership changes.
4. Validate directory dry-run and actual-change evidence.
5. Validate Company Communications testing isolation.
6. Validate email-governance mode evidence.
7. Validate catalogue and knowledge changes.
8. Validate archive and deletion controls.
9. Validate theme and layout configuration.
10. Validate audit, health, rollback, and notification evidence.


END OF FILE: 11_ADMINISTRATION_GUIDE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Access Requests and Entitlements

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-3-SCRIPT-588`

## 1. Purpose

This document describes how additional access should be requested, reviewed, granted, displayed, expired, revoked, and audited.

## 2. Access sources

A user may receive access through:

- Default access.
- Role membership.
- Direct permission.
- Temporary or governed entitlement.
- Another explicitly implemented organisational scope.

The effective-access view should explain the grant source rather than present only a combined list.

## 3. Request catalogue behaviour

When a user opens the access-request experience:

- Functions already available must be greyed out.
- Already granted access must be displayed separately.
- The display must identify whether access came from default, permission, or role.
- Governed grants must remain revocable.
- A user must not request an identical active entitlement unnecessarily.

## 4. Access request content

A request should record:

- Requester.
- Requested function or entitlement.
- Business reason.
- Current access and grant source.
- Requested scope.
- Requested duration where temporary.
- Supporting evidence where applicable.
- Submission timestamp.

The request should avoid collecting unnecessary personal information.

## 5. Review workflow

An authorised reviewer should be able to:

1. Review the requester and organisational context.
2. Review the requested function and scope.
3. See existing access and grant sources.
4. Identify conflicts or duplication.
5. Approve, reject, request information, or apply a narrower entitlement.
6. Set effective and expiry dates where applicable.
7. Record a decision reason.

The backend must revalidate the request state when an action is submitted.

## 6. Actionable notifications

Access approvals or review tasks may appear as actionable notifications. Marking the notification as read must not complete the request or remove the action.

If another reviewer has already decided the request, a stale action should fail safely and show the current result.

## 7. Grant application

Before applying a grant, the system should verify:

- The requester still exists and is eligible.
- The function still exists and is active.
- The access is not already effective through another source.
- The reviewer is authorised for the requested scope.
- Start and expiry dates are valid.
- Conflicting access is handled according to policy.

A successful grant should create durable access and audit evidence.

## 8. Temporary entitlements

Temporary access should include:

- Effective date and time.
- Expiry date and time.
- Scope.
- Approver.
- Reason.
- Automatic or governed expiry processing.
- Expiry notification where configured.
- Audit evidence.

Expiry must not remove unrelated default, role, or direct access.

## 9. Revocation

An authorised revocation should identify the exact grant being removed. Revocation must not remove access supplied independently by another source.

Revocation should record:

- Revoking actor.
- Grant source.
- Affected function and scope.
- Reason.
- Timestamp.
- Effective result.

## 10. Role and direct-permission interaction

A direct permission can coexist with role-based access. The effective-access display should make this clear so that removing one source does not create a false expectation that access has ended.

Administrators should prefer maintainable role-based grants where appropriate and use direct permissions only for justified exceptions.

## 11. Default access

Default access represents baseline functions provided to eligible users. Default functions should still appear in the granted-access view and should be identified as default.

If default access is governed by account type, department, or another organisational attribute, the rule must be documented and tested.

## 12. Segregation of duties

High-risk access may require separate request, approval, and activation responsibilities. A user should not approve their own privileged request unless an explicitly approved emergency process permits it and retains enhanced evidence.

## 13. Audit and reporting

Access reporting should support:

- Pending requests.
- Approved and rejected requests.
- Current direct grants.
- Role-derived access.
- Default access.
- Temporary access and upcoming expiry.
- Revocations.
- Reviewer and decision duration.
- Privileged access.
- Exceptions and conflicts.

## 14. Failure handling

- Duplicate submissions should not create duplicate active grants.
- Partial processing must not report complete success.
- A failed notification must not erase the access decision.
- A failed grant application should preserve the request and failure evidence.
- Retry processing must be idempotent.

## 15. Validation checklist

1. Display existing default, role, and direct access separately.
2. Confirm already available functions are greyed out.
3. Submit a valid request.
4. Reject duplicate or invalid requests.
5. Approve, reject, and request clarification.
6. Validate stale notification actions.
7. Validate temporary start and expiry.
8. Revoke one grant without removing another source.
9. Validate unauthorised review and self-approval restrictions.
10. Reconcile effective access, audit, notifications, and reports.


END OF FILE: 12_ACCESS_REQUESTS_AND_ENTITLEMENTS.md

# ATD Helpdesk Company Communications

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Status:** Communications functional and governance reference
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-4-SCRIPT-589`

## 1. Purpose

Company Communications provides governed authoring, targeting, approval, scheduling, publication, email delivery, dashboard announcements, attachments, conversations, and delivery evidence.

## 2. Campaign lifecycle

The functional lifecycle includes draft, approval, scheduled, sending, sent or published, failed, cancelled, and archived conditions where supported by the active schema. A state transition must record the actor, time, reason or approval evidence, and relevant delivery information.

## 3. Authoring

A communication may contain a name, subject, plain-text body, HTML body, announcement content, schedule, audience definition, and attachments. Authoring should preserve a content snapshot so history remains accurate after delivery.

## 4. Audience definition

Audience controls must support whole-company, department, management-level, combined position-level, exclusions, selected employees where implemented, and manually entered external addresses. Audience resolution should deduplicate recipients and retain the intended audience snapshot.

## 5. Approval and scheduling

Approve and Send and Approve and Schedule are governed actions. The confirmation should show the campaign, audience, schedule, and delivery mode. The backend must revalidate the campaign and actor before changing state.

## 6. Testing and live delivery

Test delivery must be isolated from live audiences. Explicit campaign tests should use only approved test recipients. Environment-level redirection must be surfaced because it can override the intended email-governance mode.

No implementation or documentation validation should contact live recipients without explicit governed approval.

## 7. Delivery reliability status

The repository evidence identified a browser timeout risk while delivery was processed synchronously. The delivery-reliability batch is still in progress and must not be marked complete until validated.

The target design includes:

- Fast server acknowledgement.
- Background delivery independent of the browser connection.
- One authoritative active operation per campaign.
- Recipient-level claiming and durable outcomes.
- Safe retry without resending confirmed deliveries.
- Recovery from interrupted processing.
- Queued, processing, completed, partial, and failed evidence.

## 8. Announcement history

History should show full content, resolved audience, lifecycle timestamps, recipient outcomes, provider, attempt time, failure details, and environment-redirect evidence. History should remain refreshable while processing.

## 9. Dashboard announcements

Published announcements may appear on employee and agent landing pages when the current user belongs to the authorised audience. Read state and optional governed conversations may be supported.

## 10. Attachments

Communication attachments must be stored and downloaded through authorised endpoints. Email delivery through SMTP or Microsoft Graph should retain compatible attachment evidence.

## 11. Conversations and moderation

Announcement conversations, when enabled, require authorised participation, timestamps, moderation controls, and clear separation from ticket conversations.

## 12. Branding boundary

Portal themes may include Glassmorphism. Email templates use Outlook-compatible ATD branding and must not depend on transparency, backdrop blur, or portal layout effects.

## 13. Audit and reporting

Evidence should include creator, approver, scheduler, sender, cancellation actor, audience snapshot, recipient totals, outcomes, timestamps, and failure information.

## 14. Validation checklist

1. Validate each audience type and exclusion.
2. Validate internal and external deduplication.
3. Validate approval and scheduling permissions.
4. Validate test-recipient isolation.
5. Validate environment-redirect evidence.
6. Validate browser-disconnect and retry behaviour.
7. Validate recipient-level idempotency.
8. Validate attachments through SMTP and Microsoft Graph.
9. Validate dashboard audience restrictions.
10. Validate complete history and audit evidence.


END OF FILE: 13_COMPANY_COMMUNICATIONS.md
