# ATD Helpdesk Email-to-Ticket and Email Governance

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-4-SCRIPT-589`

## 1. Purpose

This document describes outbound email governance and inbound Email-to-Ticket processing. The two capabilities share email infrastructure but have distinct data and security requirements.

## 2. Email-governance modes

The effective outbound mode should be visible as Disabled, Testing, or Live.

- **Disabled:** Delivery is skipped with durable evidence.
- **Testing:** Delivery is redirected only to approved test recipients.
- **Live:** The authoritative intended recipients are used.

An environment-level `EMAIL_REDIRECT_ALL` value can override the intended mode and must be shown in health and delivery evidence.

## 3. Outbound delivery

Outbound delivery may use SMTP or Microsoft Graph. Evidence should include intended recipient, effective recipient where redirected, provider, attempt time, result, failure, governance mode, and redirect state.

## 4. Safe testing

Testing must not contact live audiences. Test subjects and content may identify the intended recipient, but personal information should be limited. Switching to Live requires governed approval and environment verification.

## 5. Email-to-Ticket intake

Inbound processing should identify new requests and replies to existing tickets. It should preserve sender, recipients, subject, received timestamp, message identifier, conversation reference, and safe body content.

## 6. Ticket-reference matching

A reply should be associated with an existing ticket only when the reference is valid and authorised processing rules are satisfied. Ambiguous references should not update an unrelated ticket.

## 7. Duplicate prevention

Inbound processing must use durable identifiers or equivalent evidence to stop a retried mailbox read from creating duplicate tickets, replies, attachments, activity, or notifications.

## 8. Requester reply versus internal note

Inbound messages from the requester or authorised external participant should default to requester-visible conversation. Internal notes require an explicit trusted internal workflow and must never be inferred only from a subject phrase supplied by an external sender.

## 9. Attachments and content safety

Inbound attachments require size, type, storage, malware-control, and authorisation handling. Remote content and unsafe HTML should not be rendered without sanitisation.

## 10. Failure and retry

Mailbox retrieval, parsing, ticket matching, attachment storage, activity creation, and notification delivery should have separate outcomes. Retry must be idempotent and must preserve failure evidence.

## 11. Operational evidence

Health and reporting should identify last successful poll, mailbox or connector identity without secrets, processed count, duplicate count, failure count, and oldest unresolved failure.

## 12. Validation checklist

1. Create a ticket from a valid inbound message.
2. Match a valid reply to an existing ticket.
3. Reject ambiguous and invalid references safely.
4. Verify duplicate-message protection.
5. Verify requester-visible and internal-note isolation.
6. Verify sender, recipient, and timestamps.
7. Verify attachments and unsafe-content handling.
8. Verify Disabled, Testing, and Live outbound modes.
9. Verify environment redirect evidence.
10. Verify retry, audit, notification, and reporting evidence.


END OF FILE: 14_EMAIL_TO_TICKET_AND_EMAIL_GOVERNANCE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Microsoft Directory and Graph

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-4-SCRIPT-589`

## 1. Purpose

Microsoft integration supports directory synchronisation and Microsoft Graph-based email or related authorised functions. Exact tenant permissions and enabled operations are deployment configuration, not repository assumptions.

## 2. Directory synchronisation

Directory synchronisation may retrieve account and organisational attributes such as display information, email, department, position, manager context, account state, and profile media where authorised.

## 3. No-change write protection

The system must compare current and proposed values before writing. Accounts with no actual changes must not receive update writes. This reduces noise, risk, and false audit events.

## 4. Change evidence

An actual change should record account, changed fields, previous and new values where policy permits, actor or system process, timestamp, result, and failure details.

## 5. Weekly alert process

Actual directory-account changes should be consolidated into Monday 08:00 weekly alerts with actor details and included with the governed ATD Helpdesk Weekly Report process.

## 6. Microsoft Graph email

Graph email delivery should retain the same governance evidence as SMTP: intended recipient, effective recipient, mode, redirect state, provider, attempt time, result, attachments, and failure information.

## 7. Permissions and least privilege

Application permissions and delegated permissions must be limited to approved functions. Secret or certificate material must not appear in reports, source documentation, browser responses, or health output.

## 8. Error handling

Throttling, token failure, permission denial, invalid recipients, directory conflicts, and partial results require clear retry and failure evidence. Retry must avoid duplicate email or directory writes.

## 9. Profile media

Directory-sourced or locally-managed avatars should use a consistent current-profile source, cache refresh, and fallback behaviour. Media access must be authorised.

## 10. Validation checklist

1. Validate read-only directory retrieval.
2. Validate no-change accounts produce no writes.
3. Validate a controlled actual change and audit evidence.
4. Validate manager, department, and position mapping.
5. Validate Graph permission failure handling.
6. Validate throttling and retry behaviour.
7. Validate Graph email testing isolation.
8. Validate attachment delivery evidence.
9. Validate weekly change-alert content and timing.
10. Verify secrets are absent from reports and health output.

# ATD Helpdesk External System Integrations

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-4-SCRIPT-589`

## 1. Purpose

This document records the main external-system boundaries identified in the repository. An integration foundation does not prove that the integration is enabled or production validated.

## 2. Integration principles

- Define an authoritative source for each field.
- Use least-privilege credentials.
- Do not expose secrets in logs or reports.
- Make retry idempotent.
- Preserve correlation identifiers and timestamps.
- Record partial failure.
- Avoid silent destructive synchronisation.
- Provide health evidence without sensitive content.

## 3. Freshservice historical integration

Freshservice archive, import, and merge functions support historical records and transition work. Freshservice history is separate from the current ATD Helpdesk ticket lifecycle.

Import or merge processing should preserve source identifiers, avoid duplicates, retain timestamps, and report conflicts. Current-ticket archive, restore, and deletion must not imply changes to Freshservice history unless explicitly implemented.

## 4. Microsoft SQL Server production data

A Microsoft SQL Server connection layer supports production-related data or reporting. PostgreSQL remains the primary ATD Helpdesk application store unless a documented contract states otherwise.

Queries should be parameterised, scoped, observable, and protected from unintended write access. Production-data synchronisation requires a defined owner, schedule, mapping, and recovery process.

## 5. Microsoft Graph and directory

Microsoft Graph and directory processing are documented separately in `15_MICROSOFT_DIRECTORY_AND_GRAPH.md`.

## 6. SMTP

SMTP provides an outbound email path governed by Disabled, Testing, and Live controls. Provider failure and redirection evidence should be durable.

## 7. Email-to-Ticket

Inbound mailbox processing is documented separately in `14_EMAIL_TO_TICKET_AND_EMAIL_GOVERNANCE.md`.

## 8. Microsoft Teams roadmap

The roadmap includes assignment-related prompts and ticket-chat synchronisation. Teams synchronisation should preserve ticket reference, participants, message timestamps, direction, and conversation evidence while respecting internal-note and requester-visibility boundaries.

Teams capability must not be marked deployed without verified source, permissions, validation, and production evidence.

## 9. Asset and configuration records

Existing CMDB functionality and data must be preserved while the portal is rebranded using an approved replacement name. Integration references, routes where appropriate, permissions, reports, notifications, and documentation must be updated without deleting capability.

## 10. Health and monitoring

Each enabled integration should provide last success, last failure, backlog or pending count, retry state, connector identity without secrets, and actionable failure detail.

## 11. Change management

Integration changes require source backup, configuration review, dependency analysis, safe test mode, validation of negative paths, rollback planning, and tracker/documentation updates.

## 12. Validation checklist

1. Identify each enabled integration and owner.
2. Verify credentials are least privilege and not exposed.
3. Verify source-to-target field mapping.
4. Verify duplicate and retry controls.
5. Verify partial failure handling.
6. Verify timestamps and correlation evidence.
7. Verify read/write boundaries.
8. Verify health and alerting.
9. Verify rollback and recovery.
10. Verify production enablement separately from source presence.


END OF FILE: 16_EXTERNAL_SYSTEM_INTEGRATIONS.md

# ATD Helpdesk Frontend Architecture

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-5-SCRIPT-590`


## 1. Purpose

The frontend provides the browser experience for employees, agents, managers, administrators, and other authorised users. The verified architecture uses React with Vite and consumes backend REST APIs under the application API boundary.

## 2. Responsibilities

- Route and page composition.
- Role-aware navigation and workspace presentation.
- Forms, tables, cards, queues, filters, dialogs, previews, and dashboards.
- Notification and activity interactions.
- Local presentation state and safe refresh behaviour.
- API requests through shared service clients.
- Responsive and accessible portal styling.

Frontend visibility is not an authorisation boundary. Every governed action must still be validated by the backend.

## 3. Application structure

The active `frontend/src` tree is the primary frontend source. Historic releases, generated distributions, reports, and backup copies are secondary evidence.

Typical layers include:

- Application entry and route composition.
- Page-level components.
- Reusable feature components.
- Dashboard and ticket components.
- Shared API services.
- Authentication and current-user context.
- Theme, profile, and preference handling.
- Utility functions and shared styling.

## 4. Routing

Protected routes should resolve the current user before presenting governed content. Deep links must survive browser refresh and return users safely after authentication. The portal base path must remain consistent with production routing.

Back to Admin and Back to Dashboard controls should be standardised across relevant pages.

## 5. State management

State should be kept as close as practical to the feature that owns it. Shared or persistent state may include authentication, current profile, themes, notification counts, dashboard layouts, and authorised preferences.

Live refresh should preserve filters, drafts, selections, active ticket context, and scroll position where supported.

## 6. API client

Shared API clients should provide a consistent base URL, credentials or token handling, error normalisation, timeouts, and response processing. Long-running server work should return an accepted operation rather than depend on one browser request remaining open.

A client timeout does not prove that server processing failed. UI messages must distinguish timeout, accepted processing, completed work, and failed work.

## 7. Error handling

User-facing errors should be concise and actionable. Repeated failures from one click must not create duplicate banners and toasts.

### Known priority defect

Notification Centre assignment alerts currently show valid-looking ticket references but the Open action reports: `This notification does not contain a valid ticket action.` This requires payload/reference repair and a safe ticket-opening fallback immediately after documentation.

## 8. Tickets and workspaces

Ticket pages and components should support list and detail views, broad filtering, assignment, activity, replies, internal notes, attachments, archive lifecycle, priority queues, bulk actions, and authorised previews.

## 9. Dashboards

Dashboard components may provide widgets, templates, announcements, priority queues, ticket previews, statistics, and layout customisation. Named custom layouts should be managed with themes and appearance preferences.

## 10. Themes and Glassmorphism

Glassmorphism is an optional portal theme and layout style only. The portal should use restrained transparency, accessible contrast, responsive surfaces, and fallback behaviour when blur or transparency is unavailable. Glassmorphism must not be applied to email templates.

## 11. Accessibility and responsiveness

- Preserve keyboard access and visible focus.
- Use semantic controls and labels.
- Maintain sufficient contrast.
- Avoid decorative effects that impair readability.
- Support desktop, tablet, and mobile layouts.
- Provide alternatives to hover-only interactions.

## 12. Frontend validation

1. Run the production build.
2. Validate route refresh and authentication return paths.
3. Test each role and unauthorised path.
4. Validate responsive layouts.
5. Validate Light/Dark and supported themes.
6. Validate error, empty, loading, and partial states.
7. Validate filters, drafts, selection, and refresh preservation.
8. Validate attachment previews and downloads.
9. Validate Notification Centre navigation and governed actions.
10. Confirm no live email is triggered during UI validation.


END OF FILE: 17_FRONTEND_ARCHITECTURE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Backend API and Service Architecture

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-5-SCRIPT-590`


## 1. Purpose

The backend provides authentication, authorisation, business rules, persistence, audit evidence, integration orchestration, background processing, and REST APIs for the portal.

## 2. Architecture

The verified backend uses Node.js and Express. Current `backend/src` is the primary source. Route modules expose API operations, middleware establishes identity and policy checks, and services implement reusable business behaviour.

## 3. API boundary

APIs are exposed beneath `/api`. Endpoints should:

1. Authenticate the caller.
2. Validate role, permission, and scope.
3. Validate request input.
4. Revalidate the current entity state.
5. Delegate business logic to an appropriate service.
6. Commit durable state atomically where practical.
7. Create activity or audit evidence.
8. Return a clear status and safe response.

## 4. Authentication and authorisation

Backend checks are authoritative. Protected functions must not rely on hidden buttons or route guards alone. Scope may depend on ownership, department, support group, assignment, management context, role, or direct permission.

## 5. Service layer

Services should contain reusable logic for tickets, communication, email, notifications, attachments, archive, directory, reporting, and other domains. Services should avoid embedding transport-specific response handling when reusable domain results are sufficient.

## 6. Transactions and consistency

Multi-step operations should use database transactions where partial completion would corrupt business state. External side effects such as email cannot always be rolled back, so recipient claims, idempotency, durable outcome records, and recovery states are required.

## 7. Background processing

Schedulers or workers support reminders, inbound email, Company Communications, and synchronisation. Background work requires:

- Durable operation state.
- Idempotency.
- Bounded retry.
- Recovery of interrupted work.
- Correlation and timestamps.
- Failure evidence.
- Safe process restart behaviour.

## 8. Notification actions

Notification records must distinguish navigation targets from governed pending actions. Ticket-opening metadata should be compatible with current and legacy notifications. Governed operations such as assignment acceptance must revalidate backend state.

## 9. Attachments and media

File routes must validate access, normalise paths, prevent traversal, enforce size and type rules, and avoid exposing storage locations. Inline rendering must be limited to safe supported content.

## 10. Audit and observability

Important operations should preserve actor, target, timestamp, result, reason, before/after information where appropriate, and correlation identifiers. Logs and health responses must not expose secrets.

## 11. Error model

Use appropriate HTTP status codes and safe messages. Validation, authorisation, conflict, not-found, accepted-processing, and server failure must be distinguishable. A failure response must not claim that a durable action was completed.

## 12. Backend validation

1. Run syntax or automated checks.
2. Validate migrations against the expected schema.
3. Test authorised, unauthorised, and out-of-scope requests.
4. Test transaction rollback.
5. Test duplicate and retry behaviour.
6. Test background recovery.
7. Test audit and activity evidence.
8. Test file authorisation.
9. Test email and integration modes without live recipients.
10. Test notification navigation and pending actions.


END OF FILE: 18_BACKEND_API_AND_SERVICE_ARCHITECTURE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Database and Migrations

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-5-SCRIPT-590`


## 1. Purpose

PostgreSQL is the primary ATD Helpdesk application database. Repository migrations define schema evolution. A separate Microsoft SQL Server integration supports production-related data or reporting and is not assumed to be the primary application store.

## 2. Source of truth

The production schema must be verified from the production database and migration evidence. A migration file in the repository does not prove that the migration was applied.

## 3. Core data domains

The database contains or supports domains for users, roles, permissions, access requests, tickets, assignments, comments, activity, notifications, attachments, communications, deliveries, announcements, audit, archive lifecycle, preferences, catalogue, knowledge, support groups, and integration state.

Exact table and column definitions remain authoritative in the active migration and source files.

## 4. Migration rules

- Use sequential, reviewable migration files.
- Avoid destructive changes without backup and approved recovery.
- Preserve existing data during rebranding or refactoring.
- Make repeat execution safe where practical.
- Record applied migration evidence.
- Separate schema change from live data repair when appropriate.
- Validate indexes, constraints, defaults, and foreign keys.

## 5. Transactions

Schema and data changes should use transactions where supported. Large or locking operations require a deployment plan, expected duration, and rollback route.

## 6. Constraints and indexes

Constraints should enforce valid relationships and uniqueness where business rules require it. Indexes should support ticket queues, reference lookup, notification retrieval, activity ordering, recipient delivery state, audit queries, and reporting without unnecessary write overhead.

## 7. Idempotency

Durable identifiers should protect against duplicate inbound messages, duplicate communication recipients, repeated assignment actions, and repeated migrations. `ON CONFLICT` handling must match the intended business key and must not silently hide incorrect data.

## 8. Archive and deletion

Archive is not deletion. Pending deletion, restoration, permanent deletion, dependent records, and tombstone evidence require explicit schema and service support. Permanent deletion must retain the evidence required by policy.

## 9. Communication delivery state

Communication processing requires durable campaign and recipient states. The in-progress delivery-reliability design must be validated before it is documented as complete.

## 10. Backup and recovery

Before deployment migrations:

1. Verify the target environment and database.
2. Capture a usable backup.
3. Record backup location and integrity evidence.
4. Apply migrations in order.
5. Validate schema and critical queries.
6. Retain rollback or recovery instructions.

## 11. Sensitive data

Database credentials, tokens, secrets, and private keys must not be committed or included in reports. Personal information must be accessed and retained only for authorised purposes.

## 12. Migration validation

- Confirm pending and applied migration lists.
- Confirm schema objects and constraints.
- Test application startup and critical routes.
- Test legacy data compatibility.
- Verify no unintended row changes.
- Verify indexes and query behaviour.
- Verify backup and restore procedures.
- Record the final migration state in deployment evidence.


END OF FILE: 19_DATABASE_AND_MIGRATIONS.md

# ATD Helpdesk Configuration Reference

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-5-SCRIPT-590`


## 1. Purpose

This document defines configuration categories without exposing secret values. Actual environment names and required values must be verified from the active deployment configuration.

## 2. Configuration hierarchy

Configuration may come from environment variables, deployment process configuration, application defaults, database-managed settings, and authorised administrative settings. Precedence must be explicit because process-level values can override portal settings.

## 3. Core categories

### Application

- Environment name.
- Frontend and backend base paths.
- API path.
- Public portal URL.
- Logging and health behaviour.

### PostgreSQL

- Host, port, database, user, and protected credential.
- Connection pool and TLS settings.
- Migration behaviour.

### Microsoft SQL Server

- Server, database, user or approved identity, protected credential, encryption, and timeout settings.

### Authentication and sessions

- Token or session secrets.
- Cookie security.
- Expiry.
- Allowed origins and trusted proxies.

### Email

- Disabled, Testing, or Live governance mode.
- Approved test recipients.
- `EMAIL_REDIRECT_ALL` environment override.
- SMTP settings.
- Microsoft Graph settings.
- Sender identity.

### Microsoft Graph and directory

- Tenant and client identifiers.
- Protected secret or certificate references.
- Approved permissions and scopes.
- Synchronisation schedule.

### File storage

- Attachment and profile-media paths.
- Size and type limits.
- Retention and authorised download configuration.

### Background processing

- Reminder schedule.
- Email-to-Ticket polling.
- Communication scheduling.
- Directory or production synchronisation.

## 4. Secret management

Secrets must not be written to source, reports, frontend bundles, health output, or documentation. Use approved environment or secret-management facilities. Rotation must include dependency validation and rollback planning.

## 5. Email safety

Before live delivery, verify the effective process environment, governance setting, redirect recipients, sender identity, and provider connectivity. A portal setting alone is insufficient evidence if a process-level redirect remains active.

## 6. Environment separation

Development, test, staging, and production should use separate endpoints, credentials, storage, databases, and recipient safeguards where practical. Test data and test email recipients must not be confused with production audiences.

## 7. Configuration changes

A governed change should record current value category, proposed change, environment, actor, reason, validation, backup or rollback, and result. Secret values should be redacted.

## 8. Configuration validation checklist

1. Identify the effective source and precedence.
2. Confirm mandatory values exist.
3. Confirm secrets are protected.
4. Confirm database and storage targets.
5. Confirm portal and API paths.
6. Confirm email mode and redirect state.
7. Confirm Microsoft permissions.
8. Confirm schedules and time zone.
9. Confirm health output is safe.
10. Record environment-specific evidence outside source control where required.


END OF FILE: 20_CONFIGURATION_REFERENCE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Deployment and Operations

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-5-SCRIPT-590`


## 1. Purpose

Deployment is a governed process that moves a validated milestone to the server while preserving backup, migration, activation, health, and rollback evidence.

## 2. Milestone model

Feature testing is deferred until the development milestone is complete. The milestone then receives one comprehensive test, one controlled server push, and one consolidated report covering the milestone changes.

## 3. Predeployment checks

- Confirm scope and tracker status.
- Confirm the exact commit and branch.
- Review the working tree and exclude unrelated files.
- Run frontend and backend validation.
- Review migrations.
- Verify environment and email safety.
- Prepare server and database backups.
- Define rollback and acceptance criteria.

## 4. Git discipline

Stage only the intended milestone files. Do not broadly stage backups, reports, generated distributions, secrets, or unrelated local changes. Record the exact commit pushed to the controlled branch.

## 5. Server deployment

A controlled deployment may include:

1. Verify remote identity and paths.
2. Capture application and database backups.
3. Fetch or stage the exact release.
4. Install verified dependencies.
5. Apply approved migrations.
6. Build or activate frontend assets.
7. Restart the managed backend process.
8. Validate health and application routes.
9. Verify production state and logs.
10. Retain rollback evidence.

## 6. Process management

The production Node.js service is managed through the approved process manager. Restart and health evidence should identify the application, process state, start time, and final health result without exposing secrets.

## 7. Reverse proxy and deep links

The web server must support the portal base path, API proxying, static assets, and single-page application deep-link fallback. Authentication return paths and browser refresh must remain functional.

## 8. Migration handling

Migrations require a verified target database, backup, ordered execution, failure handling, and post-migration checks. A partial migration or activation must be diagnosed before repeated deployment attempts.

## 9. Postdeployment validation

- Sign in with appropriate test roles.
- Validate landing pages and routes.
- Validate ticket and notification critical paths.
- Validate attachments and static assets.
- Validate database and migration state.
- Validate scheduled work without live side effects.
- Review logs and health.
- Compare deployed commit and expected assets.

## 10. Rollback

Rollback may require application release restoration, frontend asset restoration, process restart, configuration restoration, and database recovery. Database rollback must follow the approved migration and backup strategy.

## 11. Operational reporting

The milestone report should identify scope, scripts, files, validation, commit, deployment, migrations, production checks, unresolved issues, rollback evidence, and tracker updates.

## 12. Current priority after documentation

Repair the production Notification Centre defect where assignment-alert Open actions report an invalid ticket action. The repair must be source collected, guarded, validated, deployed, and included in the next operational report.


END OF FILE: 21_DEPLOYMENT_AND_OPERATIONS.md

# ATD Helpdesk Troubleshooting and Failure Modes

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-5-SCRIPT-590`


## 1. Purpose

This guide provides a structured way to investigate failures without repeatedly applying speculative repairs.

## 2. Troubleshooting method

1. Record the exact symptom, time, user scope, and environment.
2. Reproduce safely.
3. Collect complete current source and dependencies.
4. Identify the responsible layer.
5. Capture logs and durable state.
6. Form a testable cause.
7. Apply the smallest guarded repair.
8. Validate positive, negative, and regression paths.
9. Preserve backup and rollback.
10. Update reports, trackers, and documentation.

If repeated script repairs fail, use an alternate guarded implementation route and then resume sequential scripting.

## 3. Authentication and routing failures

Check portal base path, API availability, current-user request, cookies or tokens, reverse-proxy rules, deep-link fallback, and post-login return paths.

## 4. Frontend build failures

Capture the first substantive error, file, line, and import. Avoid treating all later cascade errors as separate causes. Verify source drift before automated patching.

## 5. API failures

Check request payload, authentication, permissions, scope, route logs, database error, transaction outcome, and response status. Confirm that the browser error matches the durable server state.

## 6. Database and migration failures

Verify the target database, applied migrations, locks, permissions, syntax, constraints, and backup state. Do not repeatedly apply a partially completed migration without reconciliation.

## 7. Email failures

Check effective governance mode, `EMAIL_REDIRECT_ALL`, sender identity, provider configuration, recipient resolution, delivery record, provider response, and retry state. Do not test with live recipients during repair.

## 8. Browser timeout with completed server work

A browser timeout does not prove failure. Inspect campaign or ticket state, delivery rows, audit evidence, and logs before retrying. Retrying without idempotency can duplicate external side effects.

## 9. Notification Centre invalid ticket action

### Symptom

Assignment alerts display ticket references and an Open button, but selecting Open produces `Action not completed: This notification does not contain a valid ticket action.` Multiple error toasts and a dashboard warning may appear.

### Priority

Repair immediately after documentation.

### Investigation checklist

- Compare current and legacy notification payloads.
- Inspect action type, entity type, entity ID, ticket ID, and ticket reference.
- Inspect frontend action parsing and Open-button dispatch.
- Inspect backend notification serialization.
- Separate navigation from governed assignment actions.
- Add a safe ticket-opening fallback when a valid ticket reference or ID exists.
- Revalidate acceptance or rejection actions on the backend.
- Prevent duplicate error toasts from one click.
- Test assignment, approval, discussion, access-request, and informational notifications.

## 10. Attachment failures

Check authorisation, ticket relationship, stored path, file existence, content type, size, preview support, and proxy or static-delivery rules.

## 11. Avatar failures

Check current profile media reference, authorisation, cache-busting value, stale browser cache, fallback rendering, and consistency across portal surfaces.

## 12. Deployment failures

Identify the exact stage: push, remote fetch, backup, migration, dependency installation, build, asset activation, process restart, health, or verification. Resume only from a reconciled safe checkpoint.

## 13. Failure evidence template

Record:

- Date and time.
- Environment.
- User or role scope.
- Exact action.
- Expected and actual result.
- Screenshot or console evidence.
- Relevant request or correlation ID.
- Logs and database state.
- Suspected layer.
- Backup and rollback state.
- Repair script and validation result.

## 14. Escalation criteria

Escalate when there is data loss risk, unauthorised access, live-email risk, repeated external side effects, failed backup, unknown production state, migration inconsistency, or inability to restore service safely.


END OF FILE: 22_TROUBLESHOOTING_AND_FAILURE_MODES.md

# ATD Helpdesk Route and Function Reference

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-6-SCRIPT-591`


## 1. Purpose

This document defines how the detailed route and function catalogue should be maintained. Exact paths, methods, middleware, permissions, and exports remain authoritative in current source.

## 2. Frontend route catalogue

For each frontend route, record:

- Route path and portal base path.
- Page or component entry point.
- Authentication requirement.
- Role, permission, ownership, department, or support-group scope.
- Main user actions.
- API dependencies.
- Deep-link and refresh behaviour.
- Current implementation status.

Primary functional route groups include authentication, employee landing, operations dashboard, ticket monitor, ticket workspace, ticket detail, workspaces, asset or configuration records, ticket archive, Freshservice import, administration, profile, communications, notifications, knowledge, catalogue, onboarding, and access requests.

## 3. Backend API catalogue

For each endpoint, record:

- HTTP method and path.
- Route module.
- Authentication middleware.
- Required permission and scope.
- Request parameters and payload.
- Response and notable status codes.
- Service and database dependencies.
- Side effects, notifications, activity, and audit.
- Idempotency and retry behaviour.

## 4. Service-function catalogue

For each service function, record:

- Module and exported function.
- Functional purpose.
- Inputs and outputs.
- Transaction boundary.
- External side effects.
- Error behaviour.
- Callers.
- Testing and recovery requirements.

## 5. Background functions

Catalogue reminder schedules, inbound email polling, Company Communications scheduling and delivery, directory synchronisation, production-data synchronisation, and other recurring jobs. Record startup behaviour, lock or claim strategy, retry, recovery, and monitoring.

## 6. Notification functions

Notification creation and consumption require special mapping for:

- Notification type.
- Entity type and identifier.
- Ticket identifier and ticket reference.
- Navigation target.
- Governed action type.
- Pending-action state.
- Compatibility version or legacy shape.

Navigation to a ticket must not require a pending governed action object. Assignment acceptance or rejection must remain a separate backend-revalidated action.

## 7. Documentation method

The complete route-and-function inventory should be generated from current source after the urgent notification repair and reconciled against runtime route registration. Generated lists must be reviewed for aliases, disabled modules, legacy endpoints, and environment-specific registration.

## 8. Current limitations

This foundational reference does not claim that every historic route or backup copy is active. Current source and runtime registration take precedence. Production availability requires deployment verification.

## 9. Validation checklist

1. Enumerate active frontend routes.
2. Enumerate registered backend route modules.
3. Map each endpoint to middleware and service functions.
4. Map permissions and scope.
5. Identify external side effects.
6. Identify background entry points.
7. Identify legacy aliases.
8. Verify deep links and API paths.
9. Verify notification target and action mapping.
10. Reconcile the catalogue after deployment.


END OF FILE: 23_ROUTE_AND_FUNCTION_REFERENCE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Security and Privacy Controls

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-6-SCRIPT-591`


## 1. Purpose

This document records the security and privacy controls expected across ATD Helpdesk. It is not a substitute for an approved organisational security policy or formal security assessment.

## 2. Identity and authentication

- Require authenticated access to protected functions.
- Use approved session or token controls.
- Protect credentials and secrets.
- Enforce secure cookie and transport settings where applicable.
- Record account approval, onboarding, and acceptance state.
- Terminate or restrict invalid and inactive accounts.

## 3. Authorisation

The backend must enforce roles, permissions, ownership, department, support-group, management, and other applicable scope. Hidden frontend controls are not sufficient.

High-impact actions require stronger controls, including restricted roles, explicit confirmation, reason capture, current-state validation, and durable audit evidence.

## 4. Least privilege and access governance

Access should be granted through maintainable roles where appropriate. Direct and temporary grants require justification, visibility, expiry or revocation, and audit evidence. Effective access must show the grant source.

## 5. Data protection

- Collect only information required for support and operations.
- Restrict personal information to authorised users.
- Keep internal notes separate from requester-visible content.
- Avoid placing passwords or secrets in tickets.
- Redact secrets from logs, health output, reports, and documentation.
- Preserve required retention and deletion evidence.

## 6. Application security

- Validate input at the backend.
- Use parameterised database queries.
- Encode or sanitise untrusted output.
- Protect against path traversal and unsafe file rendering.
- Apply content and size controls to uploads.
- Use safe error messages.
- Maintain dependency and build review.

## 7. Email and communications safety

Testing and live recipients must remain isolated. Effective email governance and environment redirection must be visible. Duplicate processing must not resend confirmed communications. Whole-company communication is a high-impact action.

## 8. Integration security

Microsoft Graph, directory, SMTP, SQL Server, Freshservice, Teams, and other integrations require approved identities and least-privilege permissions. Connector secrets must not be committed or included in shareable reports.

## 9. Audit and monitoring

Record significant authentication, access, ticket, communication, directory, archive, and administrative events. Audit access must itself be restricted and reviewable.

Operational monitoring should detect repeated failures, duplicate processing, unauthorised attempts, backlog, integration failure, and health degradation without exposing sensitive data.

## 10. File and media controls

Authorise every download and preview against the current user. Store files outside publicly executable locations where practical. Use safe filenames, content types, size limits, and malware controls approved by the organisation.

## 11. Privacy in notifications

Notification previews should expose only information required for the authorised user. Shared displays must not reveal restricted ticket content. Marking a notification read must not execute or cancel a pending action.

## 12. Backup and recovery

Backups must be protected, access-controlled, tested, and retained according to policy. A backup that cannot be restored is not adequate recovery evidence.

## 13. Security validation

1. Test authorised and unauthorised users.
2. Test ownership and organisational scopes.
3. Test direct API access without frontend controls.
4. Test file access and unsafe content.
5. Test secrets and error redaction.
6. Test email-mode isolation.
7. Test duplicate and retry protection.
8. Test high-impact confirmations.
9. Review dependencies and deployment configuration.
10. Record and remediate findings before production release.


END OF FILE: 24_SECURITY_AND_PRIVACY_CONTROLS.md

## 2. Urgent production defect

### Notification Centre ticket actions

**Priority:** Repair immediately after documentation.

Assignment alerts show valid-looking ticket references but Open reports: `Action not completed: This notification does not contain a valid ticket action.` The failure affects multiple alerts and creates repeated warning presentation.

Required repair areas:

- Notification action payload and reference mapping.
- Current and legacy notification compatibility.
- Safe ticket-opening fallback.
- Separation of navigation from governed pending actions.
- Backend revalidation of acceptance or rejection.
- Duplicate error-toast prevention.
- Regression testing across notification types.

## 3. In-progress communications reliability

The Company Communications batch for timeout resilience, asynchronous delivery, recipient idempotency, test/live isolation, and expanded history remains unresolved. Script 582 stopped on guarded source markers and must be completed through a safer structural patching route after the notification priority is addressed or appropriately sequenced.

## 4. Portal appearance roadmap

- Light and Dark modes.
- Glassmorphism as an optional portal theme/layout style.
- Named custom layout templates.
- Dashboard Studio integration.
- Responsive and accessible fallback styling.

Glassmorphism does not apply to email templates.

## 5. Ticket workspace roadmap

- List and Kanban workspace.
- Adaptive Ticket Preview, Statistics, and Hide Panel modes.
- Resizable or pinned panels on wide screens.
- Drawer or full-screen behaviour on smaller screens.
- Saved personal and shared views.
- Live refresh that preserves filters and drafts.
- Broader statistics and insights.

## 6. Collaboration roadmap

- Optional collaborators and employee CC picker.
- Optional support-group alerts.
- Assignee email.
- Microsoft Teams ticket-chat synchronisation.
- Complete conversation capture and timestamps.

## 7. Ticket experience roadmap

- Agent availability.
- Detailed change confirmations.
- Resolved and closed surveys.
- Expanded attachment previews and full-screen viewing.
- Expanded audit trail.
- Reopened-ticket reporting and repeat-issue indicators.

## 8. Access and administration roadmap

- Grey out functions already available.
- Separate granted-access table with default, permission, or role source.
- Governed revocation for grants.
- Department Workspace completion.
- Default and sidebar entitlement clarity.

## 9. Reporting roadmap

Weekly reporting should run Monday at 08:00 for the previous Monday-to-Sunday period and cover assignment volumes, opening patterns, response and resolution durations, SLA performance, portal usage, recurring terms, directory changes, and agent or manager access.

## 10. Postdeployment documentation roadmap

After deployment, add the modular architecture reference, Architecture Decision Record library, function rationale records, failure-mode register, and decision playbooks.

## 11. Rebranding requirement

Preserve existing CMDB functionality and data. Rebrand labels, navigation, routes where appropriate, permission wording, dashboards, reports, notifications, and documentation after a replacement name is approved.

## 12. Status governance

A roadmap item becomes implemented only after source completion and validation. It becomes deployed only after controlled release and production verification. Update the existing dedicated tracker files without replacing their structure or history.

2. Responsive Employee, Agent and Operations dashboards plus pinned and overlay panel behaviour.
3. Universal header order: Refresh, New Request, Ticket Monitor and far-right Profile Avatar without overlap.
4. Compact Ticket Detail actions, closed-ticket restrictions, governed reopening and repeat-issue reporting.
5. Assignment support-group defaults, configurable multi-group alerts and governed escalation.
6. Broader milestone testing, deployment verification and consolidated README update at the next milestone boundary.
<!-- ATD-URGENT-RELEASE-ROADMAP-20261004-END -->

# ATD Helpdesk Developer Onboarding Guide

## Document control

- **Evidence boundary:** Whole-system repository scan generated 17 September 2026
- **Source commit:** `c6457db04b3de07f411cd62d894a8119f3c8e005`
- **Generated marker:** `ATD-DOCS-GROUP-6-SCRIPT-591`


## 1. Purpose

This guide introduces the repository, guarded development method, documentation library, and operational expectations for contributors.

## 2. Start with the documentation

Read `00_DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, the system overview, technical architecture, roles and governance, relevant functional guide, deployment guide, and known limitations before changing source.

## 3. Repository areas

- `frontend/src`: active React frontend source.
- `backend/src`: active Node.js and Express backend source.
- Database migration directories: schema evolution.
- `scripts`: sequential PowerShell collectors, implementations, repairs, validation, and deployment scripts.
- `reports`: UTF-8 evidence reports and temporary backups.
- `docs`: system documentation.

Do not treat release packages or backup directories as active source.

## 4. Script convention

Scripts use the next sequential number and lowercase underscore naming. Repair scripts retain the same convention and clearly include `fix`, for example `592_fix_...ps1`. Run scripts from the repository root and provide the exact command.

Prefer one consolidated report per script. Do not create routine empty stderr reports or unnecessary availability checks.

## 5. Guarded workflow

1. Confirm the current repository state.
2. Collect complete source and dependencies into a UTF-8 TXT report.
3. Review exact source before proposing changes.
4. Create timestamped backups.
5. Refuse unexpected source drift.
6. Apply minimal targeted changes.
7. Validate syntax, build, and relevant behaviour.
8. Roll back automatically on failure where practical.
9. Record one consolidated report.
10. Update existing trackers at the appropriate boundary.

If repeated exact-marker repairs fail, switch to a safer structural patching route rather than continuing a long chain of fragile repairs.

## 6. Functional batches

Implement future feature work in coherent groups of four tightly related functions. Testing is deferred until the milestone implementation is complete, followed by one comprehensive milestone test, one controlled server push, and one consolidated report.

Targeted repair validation remains appropriate for a production defect.

## 7. Safety boundaries

- Do not send live email during development validation.
- Do not write directory values when no change exists.
- Do not remove CMDB functionality or data during rebranding.
- Do not stage unrelated files.
- Do not expose secrets in reports.
- Do not bypass backend authorisation.
- Do not assume a migration is applied because the file exists.

## 8. Local validation

Use the repository's defined frontend build and backend checks. Validate role and permission behaviour, negative paths, migrations, files, scheduled work, email mode, notifications, and durable evidence appropriate to the change.

## 9. Documentation and tracker updates

Update the dedicated development tracker, JSON tracker, and capability register consistently. Preserve filenames, structure, sections, and history. Update the Project README at the milestone boundary.

## 10. Current handoff

- Documentation Groups 1 through 6 are being created through Scripts 586 to 591.
- The immediate engineering priority after documentation is the Notification Centre invalid ticket-action defect.
- Company Communications asynchronous delivery remains in progress.
- The next script after Script 591 is reserved for notification-defect source collection.

## 11. First repair task

The Notification Centre collector should gather complete source for notification creation, database shape, API serialization, frontend display, action dispatch, ticket routes, assignment acceptance, and relevant migrations. The collector must produce one shareable UTF-8 TXT report and perform no source or production changes.

## 12. Definition of done

A change is complete only when scope, source, validation, evidence, tracker state, deployment state, and unresolved risks are accurately recorded. Local implementation must not be described as production deployment.


END OF FILE: 26_DEVELOPER_ONBOARDING_GUIDE.md

# ATD Helpdesk Development Tracker

> Living engineering tracker. Update this file at each script completion, milestone transition, failure, repair, validation, server push, and README release.

## Status legend

- `DONE`: implemented and validated at the required level
- `IN PROGRESS`: active development batch
- `READY`: requirements and dependencies are available
- `BLOCKED`: cannot proceed until a recorded condition is resolved
- `DEFERRED`: intentionally scheduled for a later milestone
- `TEST PENDING`: installation passed, consolidated functional validation remains

## Operating rules

1. Continue PowerShell script numbering sequentially under `scripts`.
2. Preserve current working files with exact SHA-256 validation before changes.
3. Create timestamped backups and automatic rollback paths.
4. Keep email disabled and background jobs disabled during controlled development unless a governed email test explicitly requires otherwise.
5. Record participant, activity, and timestamp for ticket changes.
6. Prioritize brief floating activity popups. Show Undo only when a transactional inverse exists.
7. Run one consolidated functional test at the end of each development milestone.
8. Push to the server only after milestone validation.
9. Produce one consolidated milestone report and update the Project README at the milestone boundary.

## Current milestone: Email, SLA, ticket review, routing and interaction

### BATCH B - Finance Workspace Dashboard + Approval/KPI Governance (2026-11-14)

- [x] Database: work-item KPI extension migration (original/effective due dates, evidence_state, review_state, kpi_objective, kpi_period, confidentiality_level)

### Milestone objective

Deliver a reviewed ticket-submission flow, configurable priority SLA targets, governed requester/support-group/assignee communication, robust email reply routing, efficient recipient switching, priority-ordered acceptance cards, hover previews, stable list scrolling, and ticket activity popup foundations.

### Work items

- [x] `183` Source and schema checkpoint plus developer tracker
- [x] `184` Ticket review before final submission
- [x] `185` Priority SLA policy, response target, resolution target, persistence and history
- [x] `186` Requester, Support Group and proposed-assignee governed email workflow
- [x] `187` Email replies, reference matching, idempotency and recipient switching
- [x] `188` Pending acceptance cards, priority ordering and hover/focus previews
- [x] `189` Reusable sticky header, scrollable body and sticky footer list shell
- [x] `190` Ticket activity floating popup with participant and timestamp
- [x] `191` Transaction-safe Undo for supported reversible changes
- [ ] `192` Consolidated milestone validation  **NEXT**
- [ ] `193` Server push, consolidated milestone report and comprehensive README update

### Acceptance criteria

- Ticket cannot be submitted until the requester reviews the final summary.
- Ticket reference appears in all lifecycle email subjects.
- Requester, owning Support Group and proposed assignee receive the correct governed message.
- Priority determines visible response and resolution targets.
- Duplicate delivery is prevented through idempotency.
- Email replies attach to the correct ticket and retain sender, recipients and timestamps.
- Recipient changes are audited and never expose internal notes.
- Pending acceptance is ordered by priority and SLA urgency.
- Hover/focus preview shows a short description and a Read More action.
- List headers and footers remain visible while rows scroll.
- Ticket changes produce a brief floating popup and persistent activity record.
- Undo is offered only for safe, supported actions.

## Completed foundation

- Assignment and access schema foundation
- Guided routing and assignment proposal API
- Assignment acceptance controls
- Actionable notifications
- Outside-group redirection
- Collaborators and email participants
- Access requests and approvals
- Temporary entitlement activation, expiry and revocation
- Duplicate request conflict handling
- Entity-aware notification actions
- Eligibility-aware feature access
- Five-second access-workflow popups

## Deferred product integrations

- Expanded notification-history explorer
- Dedicated detailed Activity page
- Warehouse-management integration
- Health and Safety responder directory
- Department-specific workflow adapters
- Frontend bundle code splitting and manual chunks

## Risks and watch items

- Email governance must prevent accidental external delivery during development.
- SLA policy should be configurable rather than permanently hard-coded.
- IT operational eligibility requires reliable department data.
- Ticket visibility must remain requester, participant, assignment and authorised-scope based.
- Existing email-to-ticket processing and Microsoft Graph integration must remain intact.
- Undo must never delete original audit history.

## Decision log

- Floating change popup is higher priority than the expanded notification-history explorer.
- Notification history and Activity explorer remain separate concepts.
- Operations Dashboard is IT operational access, not a universal employee feature.
- Helpdesk self-service remains available across departments with strict ticket visibility.
- README updates occur at milestone boundaries, not after each individual script.

## Current priority checkpoint

Updated: 2026-09-10 20:56:56
Branch: $branch
Baseline: $head

### Priority batch now being prepared

1. **Manager approval for catalogue requests**
   - Every Service Catalogue and Asset Catalogue request requires the requester's Manager approval before operational fulfilment, except Printer Access, Password Reset, and Wi-Fi Access.
   - Catalogue cards must visibly state **Manager approval required** before submission.
   - Submission creates a pending approval request and ticket lifecycle record without presenting the item as approved work.
   - The Manager receives persistent actionable notification and governed email where enabled.
   - Approval advances the request to operational triage; rejection records the reason and informs the requester.
   - If no verified Manager is available, route to a governed fallback approval queue rather than bypass approval.

2. **Today-first Ticket Monitor**
   - Default to tickets logged today and load new tickets into the Monitor.
   - Filters: Department and Agent.
   - Date windows: Today, Yesterday, 1 day, 2-7 days, and 30 days.
   - Add a clear Show/Hide tickets older than 7 days control.
   - Preserve current filters during refresh and assignment actions.
   - Show created time, requester, department, lifecycle state, assignee, priority, and SLA.

3. **Employee transition communication**
   - Prepare a whole-company reminder directing employees to the new ATD Helpdesk.
   - Include or attach a manual guide covering ticket logging.
   - Record that access to the previous Helpdesk has been revoked by the responsible HOD.
   - Do not send automatically until audience preview, content review, and governed send confirmation are completed.

4. **Tracker continuity and milestone scan**
   - Maintain development tracker, capability register, and governance handoff.
   - Validate implementation claims against code.
   - Schedule a complete project capability scan and README reconciliation at the milestone boundary.

<!-- ATD-SCRIPT-431-LIFECYCLE-ESCALATION-START -->
## Current lifecycle, escalation, monitor and terminology checkpoint

Updated: 2026-09-10 22:30:32
Branch: feature/launch-communications-queue-compatibility
Baseline: 120e91a47d584ac25a250ee18689389624e2fa7c

### Active four-function implementation group

1. **Today-first Ticket Monitor and minimizable lifecycle columns**
   - Default range: Today.
   - Ranges: Yesterday, 1 through 7 days, and 30 days.
   - Filters: Department, Agent, support group, priority and lifecycle state.
   - Show or hide tickets older than seven days.
   - Column order: Unassigned; Awaiting Manager Approval; Awaiting Agent Acceptance; Open; Investigating; Pending; Waiting Approval; Escalated; Resolved; Closed.
   - Resolved and Closed collapsed by default; users can minimize individual columns and restore the default layout.

2. **Governed escalation workflow**
   - Escalate to verified Manager or Head of Department.
   - Mandatory reason, urgency and required action.
   - Fallback to Escalation Governance Queue when no verified recipient exists.
   - States: Requested, Awaiting Acknowledgement, Acknowledged, Returned, Resolved and Cancelled.
   - Persistent actionable notifications retain actions after read state changes.

3. **Catalogue Manager approval**
   - Manager approval required for Service Catalogue and Asset Catalogue requests.
   - Exceptions: Printer Access, Password Reset and Wi-Fi Access.
   - Catalogue items must display approval requirements before submission.
   - Approval-required requests remain outside operational fulfilment until approved.

4. **Terminology and conversation actions**
   - Open Case becomes View Ticket throughout the portal.
   - Send becomes Reply.
   - Send and Resolve becomes Reply and Resolve.
   - Send and Close becomes Reply and Close.
   - Internal-note submission becomes Add Internal Note.

### Archive rule retained

Archived tickets appear under Pending Deletion during a seven-day grace period. The grace period does not trigger automatic permanent deletion. Only a Superadministrator can perform governed permanent deletion after the grace period, with reason, typed confirmation and audit evidence.

### Validation boundary

Before implementation, validate exact current source locations for monitor columns, escalation routes, conversation buttons, notification actions and all Open Case labels. At the milestone boundary, run the full project capability scan, reconcile the tracker and README, then perform one comprehensive test.

<!-- ATD-SCRIPT-469-TICKET-ARCHIVE-START -->
## Current ticket archive lifecycle batch

Updated: 2026-09-12 14:12:35
Branch: feature/launch-communications-queue-compatibility
Baseline HEAD: 120e91a47d584ac25a250ee18689389624e2fa7c

### Current four-function group

1. Dedicated Active Tickets, Archived Tickets, and Pending Deletion views.
2. Seven-day archive grace-period calculation and visible countdown without automatic permanent deletion.
3. Governed restore and permanent-deletion controls with reasons, typed high-impact confirmation, and audit evidence.
4. Requester-first and Department-first archive presentation with structured archive activity.

### Lifecycle rules

- Active tickets have no archive timestamp.
- Archived tickets remain recoverable and searchable.
- Pending Deletion means an archived ticket is within or beyond the seven-day governance window; it does not mean automatic deletion will occur.
- After seven days, a ticket remains listed until an authorised Superadministrator deliberately performs governed permanent deletion.
- Agents may archive only tickets assigned to their own account and may not restore or permanently delete tickets.
- Managers may restore tickets only within proven governed scope.
- Superadministrators may restore and perform governed permanent deletion.
- Permanent deletion requires a reason, an impact warning, typed confirmation, and retained audit or tombstone evidence.
- Freshservice import history must remain separate from the current-ticket archive lifecycle.

### Required presentation

- Ticket reference and subject.
- Requester avatar or initials and requester name.
- Department.
- Previous status and current archive state.
- Priority.
- Archived by, archive date, archive reason, grace-period expiry, and time remaining.
- View Ticket, View Activity, Restore, and governed Delete actions according to authority.

### Operating boundary

- Development and curated GitHub push only.
- This checkpoint does not modify application source, execute migrations, send communications, or contact the server.
<!-- ATD-SCRIPT-469-TICKET-ARCHIVE-END -->
<!-- ATD-SCRIPT-470-REOPEN-STATISTICS-START -->
## Resolved-ticket recurrence and reopen statistics

- A requester must be able to reopen the same ticket when a previously resolved issue reappears, provided the ticket has not entered a protected permanent-deletion state.
- Reopening must preserve the original resolution, closure and assignment history rather than replacing prior lifecycle evidence.
- The requester must provide a recurrence reason when reopening; the action, requester, timestamp and prior status must be written to ticket activity.
- Reopening a Resolved or Closed ticket must return it to the governed active queue, clear only the current resolved/closed state timestamps where required by the existing queue model, and retain historical resolution-cycle evidence separately.
- Every reopen event must increment a server-maintained reopen count and record the latest reopened timestamp and actor.
- Ticket statistics and printable manager reporting must include reopened-ticket count, reopen rate, repeat-issue count, time from resolution to recurrence, service category, support group and anonymised recurrence themes where appropriate.
- Reopened tickets must remain visible as repeat issues in agent and manager views without exposing requester-identifying survey commentary in aggregate reports.
- Reopening must not create a duplicate ticket unless a governed split or linked-child workflow is deliberately used later.
- Closure notifications and surveys must account for reopen state so that duplicate surveys are not requested for superseded closure cycles.
- Implementation, migration, build, functional testing and deployment remain pending the guarded implementation batch and milestone validation gate.
<!-- ATD-SCRIPT-470-REOPEN-STATISTICS-END -->

1. Disable or hide actions applicable only to open tickets after closure.
2. Keep read-only details, conversation, attachments, audit, SLA, escalation and participation history available.

### Assignment and multi-group support governance

1. Selecting an assignee automatically selects the person's only eligible support group.

<!-- ATD-FINANCE-COMMERCIAL-INTELLIGENCE-20260927-BEGIN -->
## Finance Workspace commercial intelligence, reporting and security scope - 27 September 2026

### Product vision

The Finance Workspace must operate as a secure Finance and Commercial Intelligence workspace, not only as a ticket queue. Structured information captured once must support operational processing, approvals, management dashboards, Boardroom packs, clean Excel exports, reconciliations, forecasting and decision-making.

### Finance organisational model

1. **Finance Main and CFO Office** - executive Finance work, strategy, budgets, forecasts, reporting, treasury, audit, compliance, confidential correspondence and escalations.
2. **Accounts Payable Office** - supplier invoices, supplier payments, employee payments and reimbursements, sales orders, purchase-order matching, payment batches, supplier queries and payment exceptions. This office has its own manager and operational queues.
3. **Finance Boardroom** - a governed meeting and decision space for agendas, review packs, decisions, action items and point-in-time reporting snapshots. The Boardroom is not a support group or default assignee.
4. **Entra-backed organogram** - use read-only department, job title, manager, office location, company, employee type, account status and object ID references. Do not invent names or write back to Microsoft Entra ID. Suggested membership requires governed review.

### Finance process catalogue

- Supplier invoice
- Supplier payment
- Employee payment
- Employee reimbursement
- Sales order processing
- Purchase-order matching
- Payment exception
- Supplier query
- Budget review
- Financial reporting
- Audit request
- Boardroom review
- General Finance request

### Product and commercial data model

Use governed product families and line items so one invoice or order can contain multiple products:

- `RSB` - Rear Step Bumpers
- `CF` - Chassis Frames
- `TG` - Tailgates
- `BL` - Bedliners
- `BR` - Box Rails
- `EC` - Endcaps
- `OTHER` - Other or uncategorised products

Capture product family, product code, description, customer and internal part numbers, variant, programme or model, customer, site, unit of measure, quantity, unit price, discount, tax, net line value, currency, cost centre and effective dates. Preserve future mapping to Syspro or another authoritative product master.

### Structured Finance data capture

Capture dedicated fields rather than hiding reportable values in descriptions or comments. The data model must support Finance office, process, transaction type, customer, supplier, employee reference, invoice, purchase order, sales order, delivery note, payment batch, document and due dates, quantities, amounts, currency, exchange rate, local-currency value, cost centre, department, general-ledger reference, approval state, payment state, reconciliation state, confidentiality, owner, completion and reopened indicators.

### Reporting and presentation readiness

Provide Executive Finance, Product Performance, Accounts Payable, Finance Manager and Boardroom reporting. Reports must support daily, weekly, monthly, quarterly, year-to-date, financial-year and custom periods. Every chart and headline value must drill into the exact authorised records represented while preserving active filters.

Required measures include units, gross value, discount, tax, net value, local-currency value, paid and unpaid value, approved and pending value, overdue value, processing time, approval time, exceptions, reopened items, blocked payments, completion rate, on-time payment rate and invoice-to-payment cycle time.

Boardroom packs must be point-in-time snapshots containing reporting period, refresh timestamp, filters, executive summary, KPIs, charts, variances, exceptions, commentary, decisions required and underlying record count.

### Clean Excel exports

Provide governed `.xlsx` exports for detailed Finance data, product sales, Accounts Payable, approvals and management packs. Management workbooks should include Summary, Product Performance, Accounts Payable, Approvals, Overdue Items, Office Workload, Exceptions, Detailed Data, Data Dictionary and Export Metadata sheets.

Raw-data sheets must use one header row, one record per row, one attribute per column, genuine Excel dates, numeric money and percentages, separate currency columns, stable names, record IDs, source references, filters, export timestamp, classification and exporter identity. Do not use merged cells or decorative blank rows in raw-data sheets.

Every workbook must contain a Data Dictionary describing column name, business meaning, type, allowed values, mandatory status, source, classification, calculation rule and example.

### Data quality controls

Detect duplicate supplier invoices, missing supplier or amount, invalid currency, unjustified negative values, invalid date order, payment before approval, quantity without product, missing customer where required, line totals not matching document totals, tax inconsistency, missing purchase order, missing approval, missing supporting documents, invalid cost centre, invalid Finance office and conflicting confidentiality. Status values are Valid, Incomplete, Needs Review, Exception and Blocked.

### Finance security baseline

1. Enforce least privilege and separate Requester, Processor, Reviewer, Approver, Payment Releaser, Workspace Manager, Finance Security Administrator, Auditor and Boardroom Participant roles.
2. Prevent one person from requesting, processing, approving and releasing the same sensitive transaction unless an authorised exception is recorded.
3. Support confidentiality levels: Internal, Finance Restricted, Confidential, CFO Restricted, Boardroom Restricted, Payment Restricted, Employee Financial Information and Supplier Banking Information.
4. Restrict sensitive fields, previews, downloads and exports independently from general workspace visibility.
5. Record every view, download, export, approval, decision, change and access-revocation event.
6. Confidential uploads remain disabled until protected storage, extension and signature validation, generated filenames, size controls, malware scanning, CSRF protection, retention and authenticated download controls are configured and reviewed.
7. Plan Microsoft Entra MFA, Conditional Access, trusted or compliant device rules, session controls and step-up authentication through the authorised identity-security process.

### Updated four-function prototype batch

1. **Finance organisation and secure intake** - offices, organogram references, process catalogue, classifications, structured forms, product lines and data-quality validation.
2. **Finance operations workspace** - office selector, List, Kanban, Preview, Statistics, Accounts Payable, CFO, approval, overdue, exception, product and period queues.
3. **Finance lifecycle and controls** - information requests, approvals, separation of duties, payment states, exceptions, completion, reopening, restricted fields and audit history.
4. **Finance intelligence and reporting** - executive, Accounts Payable and product dashboards, Boardroom pack, drill-down reporting, clean Excel exports, data dictionary and export auditing.

### Delivery guardrails

Build a reviewable prototype with structured configuration and no fabricated financial values. Real data enters only through authorised integration or governed manual capture. Review terminology, workflow and security with Finance leadership before enabling confidential document upload or production deployment.
<!-- ATD-FINANCE-COMMERCIAL-INTELLIGENCE-20260927-END -->

### Authoritative Finance assignment rules

- Finance Workspace assignment is direct employee ownership and must not depend on IT support groups, IT queues, IT agent acceptance, or IT SLA activation.
- All verified active Finance employees must appear in Team Administration and every Finance owner, manager, backup, reviewer and approver selector.
- A missing Finance responsibility or Primary Function record must flag configuration as incomplete; it must not hide the employee.
- Initial work-specialisation recommendations must be inferred from historical Finance ticket-assignment activity. Historical activity may rank suggestions but must never remove an eligible Finance employee.
- Historical Finance assignment activity is currently empty, so new employees must display `Work profile not established` until sufficient activity exists.
- Unassigned Finance work must be visibly flagged and included in Finance Manager correction and unattended-work queues.

### Finance email-to-ticket and presentation objective

- The immediate presentation objective is a controlled, clearly labelled demonstration using an authorised external sender address.
- The demonstration must show: incoming email, governed rule match, automatic Finance ticket creation, direct Finance owner assignment, My Work visibility, completion, documentary evidence linkage, and Finance Management completion awareness.
- Demonstration records must be excluded from ordinary operational reporting unless `Include demonstration data` is enabled.
- Production mailbox addresses, HOD addresses, delegated-task mailboxes and high-risk senders must be added later through governed configuration, never hard-coded in source.
- Important Finance emails should become tickets only when governed sender/content/risk rules match. Duplicate message and conversation identifiers must be preserved.
- Completed Finance tasks must support shared-drive or SharePoint evidence references and management notification status.

### Shared catalogue requirements

- The shared workspace catalogue architecture remains authoritative for IT, Finance, Process Engineering and future workspaces.
- Every Incident and Service Request catalogue branch must contain `Other`, opening a manual untemplated request while preserving workspace and request-type context.
- Governed catalogue administration must support add, edit, enable, disable, archive/restore, workspace applicability, required fields, evidence requirements, routing and SLA references.
- Disabled catalogue items remain available for historical records but must not appear for new requests.

### Company Communications requirements

- Company Communications must support company-wide, department, management-level, combined position-level and manually entered external audiences, including exclusions.
- Monitoring must expose intended recipients, effective recipients, delivery attempts, sent/failed/skipped outcomes, first and latest read times, acknowledgements, comments and other recorded interactions.
- Campaigns without delivery records must be visibly flagged as incomplete or not processed.
- Production remains authoritative; monitoring access must follow the signed-in account's roles and scope rather than copying all server data into a personal account.
